Nathan to build a JIRA **requirements ticket** for the "scan & validate" work with the rules documented in it; [[wiki/people/aaron.md|Aaron]] to help organize Jira (Nathan + Pavel to meet him). [[raw/about_tickets_redemption-gcoam-june-18–2025.md]]

## June 19 2025 — Flex POS use case, the three schemas, the Incom three-call API, money flow

The next-morning session ([[wiki/meetings/2025-06-19-flex-pos-use-case-design.md]]; [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan Carver]], [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]]; [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]] absent) wrote the initial **Flex POS** use case for the September-1 beta. **§12: two transcriptions of the SAME meeting** were staged — a WebVTT (speaker-labelled) and a fuller narrative copy; treated as one meeting, both cited, facts once. [[raw/flex-POS_design-june-19-2025.md]] [[raw/flex_POS-use_case_architecture_requirements-june-19-2025.md]]

- **Flex POS Mutagoose capabilities (Pavel's doc, committed to the FlexPOS repo docs folder).** A device with a **10-inch touchscreen, magnetic swipe reader, ethernet + wireless (possibly 3G)**, for the legacy Yellow Board / Mutagoose; two modes — **setup mode** and **work mode**. Priority #1 is the Mutagoose (simplified) variant, not the full POS implementation. [[raw/flex_POS-use_case_architecture_requirements-june-19-2025.md]] [[raw/flex-POS_design-june-19-2025.md]]
- **Device security (new topic Pavel raised).** Because anyone with POS access could issue gift cards without a ticket (accepted for MVP), manual card-issue needs security — a **PIN, a security QR scan, a special magnetic card, or a per-operational-day PIN** delivered via mobile app / cloud. Additional capabilities: **firmware update**, a **lease feature** (set a per-POS expiration date / push a disable message from the server), **remote initial setup** (push credentials/GUI settings from the cloud), and **lock management**. [[raw/flex_POS-use_case_architecture_requirements-june-19-2025.md]]
- **Use case "Manual Ticket Redemption for FlexPOS" (target Sept-1 beta).** Redeem a Muda-Goose paper ticket (**no barcode, printed alphanumeric value**) into a prepaid **MasterCard** — a **single-charge** card (not rechargeable for one more year) complying with the **June-1-2026** Georgia legislation. Steps: player wins on a Muda-Goose-retrofitted machine (~35,000 in the state) → machine pushes data over **RS-485** (chainable machine-to-machine) to a printer in the cash cage → player states the amount won (e.g. $82) → cashier manually keys the value into the POS, takes a **blank unactivated card**, scans/swipes it → POS connects to the **Incom** cloud server, which returns a validation key and **deducts $2** → player gets **$80** on the card (one swipe). Barcode (Tito) validation and OCR are **not** MVP; positive + negative test cases to be written. [[raw/flex_POS-use_case_architecture_requirements-june-19-2025.md]]
- **Three business schemas + the Flex hardware catalog.** (1) **eWallet / temporary wallet** — player pays at a kiosk, gets a PIN-paired bar-coded ticket (no value printed) carried between machines; JVL's initial >1-year-old approach, **built and ready for positive test integration** but judged wrong for Georgia (the first touchpoint should be the machine, not a kiosk detour — so it "will not survive"). (2) **Tito.** (3) **Mutagoose** (Mutables). Hardware pieces (each prefixed **Flex-**): **Flex-Port, Flex-SMIB, Flex-Pay, Flex-Tower, Flex-POS**, plus the **Tito server** — each works across schemas. Incom is building a **digital electronic wallet** for at-machine transactions; the single-charge MasterCard architecture is expected to change (a card that can't go back into a machine is a problem). Merchant-number idea (attach a merchant number to FlexPay/FlexTower to act as merchant on-location) floated, not pursued. [[raw/flex_POS-use_case_architecture_requirements-june-19-2025.md]]
- **Personas (in the GitHub knowledge base):** player, cashier, location owner, JVL technician, **master (master license holder** = operator who buys machines and places them in locations), Incom, and **GLC (Georgia Lottery Corporation**, a crown corporation with the primary SAS port). Val wants to interview a master (**Felipe** to facilitate) but keep JVL's POS/business plans private — "a surprise to the market." [[raw/flex_POS-use_case_architecture_requirements-june-19-2025.md]]
- **Incom API = three requests.** (1) **authenticate** the terminal with credentials (**Client ID + client secret**; Terminal ID = the FlexPOS **serial number**; location code / UPC card data); (2) **pre-authorize** the card (send the amount; Incom checks the card suits the location's min/max limits); (3) **issue/authorize** the card. The auth token expires quickly. [[raw/flex_POS-use_case_architecture_requirements-june-19-2025.md]]
- **Money-settlement flow.** A $30 win → card gets **$28**, **$2** service fee (per the JVL↔Incom agreement; the fee must be disclosed to the player). **JVL pays the full card value to Incom** and must **collect the entire ticket value** ($82 example) from the location/master it contracts with. Val is pushing to settle **daily** (or within 72h / one week). Scale: **6,000+ locations, tens of millions of dollars** to front/transact. Pavel is using **Mermaid** diagrams (POS Mutable business schema, committed to the FlexPOS repo docs). [[raw/flex_POS-use_case_architecture_requirements-june-19-2025.md]]

## June 20 2025 — Flex POS use-case review: redeem/auth workflows, failure modes, capability-vs-feature doctrine

The next-day continuation of June-19 ([[wiki/meetings/2025-06-20-flex-pos-use-case-review.md]]; [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan Carver]], [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]]). [[raw/review_flex-POS_use_cases.md]]

- **Redeem-ticket workflow, tied to an auditable session.** Nathan's diagram: authenticate/authorise the operator → open a Flex-POS↔JVL-cloud session → enter ticket metadata + value → create a ticket-redemption record (device, cashier ID, authoriser, ticket ID, amount) → swipe the blank gift card → JVL cloud ↔ **Incom**: authenticate, then **pre-authorise**, then **activate** the card (Pavel's correction: the term is "activate," and the amount is on the card by then) → record success (a bounding-box **two-step in-sync** with a retry on failure) → print/email/SMS receipt. A daily batch reconciles JVL's redemption records against Incom to know how much to collect and to flag pre-authorised-but-not-completed tickets. [[raw/review_flex-POS_use_cases.md]]
- **Authentication for September-1.** Single-stage **PIN or magnetic card**; defer 2FA. Three role levels — **admin / manager / cashier** — each PIN tied to an individual and controllable from the cloud; a higher rank issues a lower rank's card (via a QR/higher-PIN two-step). Manager = cashier + reports. [[raw/review_flex-POS_use_cases.md]]
- **Val's failure-mode questions.** The POS is a **critical single point of failure** (if it fails the location stops); he raised recovery from frozen/hung transactions, two POS in parallel at larger locations and how they'd prioritise/avoid Incom collisions, a dropped/mis-issued card, a sign-off screen, and JVL's need to gate every processing (JVL must permit each transaction, not just lock the POS). Answer approach: a **failure-mode analysis** as a living document; ask **Incom's John** since Incom has seen many markets and isn't a competitor. Connections: **HTTPS to Incom, secure-WebSocket to the JVL cloud**. [[raw/review_flex-POS_use_cases.md]]
- **Cloud-as-gateway vs POS-direct debate.** Nathan proposed routing all Incom traffic through the JVL cloud (so a device can be blocked/audited centrally, device stays dumb); Pavel prefers **POS→Incom direct** so redemption still works if the cloud is down (the cloud is a lease/rights server), pushing a lock only when internet is up. Not resolved. [[raw/review_flex-POS_use_cases.md]]
- **Full-insert privilege-card provision.** Distinct from the Incom card reader: an **attendant/privilege access card** on a **lanyard**, fully inserted to keep the POS live and auto-locking when pulled (or PIN + idle-timeout lock). Not MVP, but the **housing must be molded with the opening now** (a plugged cavity later is expensive) — swipe reader always present, full-insert reader could share the same channel. [[raw/review_flex-POS_use_cases.md]]
- **The capability↔feature↔story / Jira↔GitHub doctrine (extended discussion).** Wedge a **"capability"** level between product (blue, e.g. IP 902) and feature (purple) in Jira; capabilities are long-lived buckets that "grow in fulfilment," features are ≤1-week and must **close** (branch → tested → merged to main → feature marked complete). Developers live in **GitHub** (issues/branches), not Jira stories; the product/engineering alignment happens only at the capability↔feature layer — which frees [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]] from acting as the developers' administrator. Nathan bridges use-cases→binary requirements→features and keeps the traceability between Jira and GitHub. September-1 non-market priority order: **setup → authenticate → redeem gift card → reports → lease termination.** Undertaking: Nathan/Pavel finish the electro-mechanical (functional/use-case) requirements first and hold the Incom-John discovery call Monday. [[raw/review_flex-POS_use_cases.md]]

## June 23 2025 — InComm alignment: SkillPay/PrizePay products; the cheaper cage-dispenser model reaffirmed

On the weekly [[wiki/organizations/incom.md|InComm]] alignment call ([[wiki/meetings/2025-06-23-incomm-jvl-nebraska-georgia-alignment.md]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] + [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]] with John/Alan/Adam) the payout-device strategy connected to InComm's own product roadmap — full product detail on [[wiki/organizations/incom.md]]. Points bearing on the device catalog: [[raw/incomm_jvl_alignment-june-23-2025.md]]

- **The card-back-into-play API is still unfinalized.** InComm confirmed the **secondary purchase API** (for Georgia SkillPay's two-step process, and for spending a card back at a machine via a **$40 barcode scanner + 3-digit PIN**) is not built — "we haven't finalized the product." This is the same missing ingredient (a card usable for betting inside machines) the Apr-8/11 temporary-wallet model depended on. Each machine used as a payment endpoint needs its own **terminal ID** (separate location contract), **or** a central screen/kiosk serves as **one terminal ID** — matching JVL's "one experience per location" architecture. [[raw/incomm_jvl_alignment-june-23-2025.md]]
- **Val's cheaper-than-a-kiosk model, restated to InComm.** Val reaffirmed the cage **dispenser + POS** path (validate a MudaGoose ticket at the cashier, split printer from machine, capture the signal, dispense a card with a small player-facing touchscreen for terms) — no full kiosk — consistent with the [[#the-device-variants-on-the-table-apr-8--apr-11|cage-POS device variant]] and the [[#june-11-2025--the-manual-pos-2-model-for-mother-goose-operators|manual "POS 2" Mother-Goose model]]. Val's revenue thesis (split the $3/$2 service charge with InComm, earn recurring processing revenue) is unchanged; John confirmed InComm makes no money on kiosks and only moves money + supplies card products. [[raw/incomm_jvl_alignment-june-23-2025.md]]

## June 27 2025 — Flex POS implementation planning: the September-1 beta definition & release cadence

A roadmap meeting ([[wiki/meetings/2025-06-27-flex-pos-implementation-planning.md]]; [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan Carver]] leading, [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]]). [[raw/Flex-POS Implementation Planning.md]]

- **September-1 non-market beta, precisely defined.** A **live Incom test site** in JVL's Georgia showroom emulating a location: three machines — **Flex S, an IGT (most prevalent in the market), and a vanilla machine** — all fitted with **Mother Goose** hardware, cable to a **counter printer**, and a **Flex POS** on the counter (3D-printed plastics acceptable if the molds aren't ready). Success = play a game, trigger a payout, the printer prints a ticket, the cashier **manually** keys the value (e.g. $100) into the POS and, through the Incom pairing cycle, issues a prepaid MasterCard verified at ~$98 (after the $2 fee). Setup expected to start ~third week of August; borrow machines from an operator (they work with **United**, which has largely moved to the "access" system — Mother Goose still ~90%+ of the market). [[raw/Flex-POS Implementation Planning.md]]
- **Hardware status.** The **ARM board arrived** (a week to validate); Eugeny installs out-of-box software then Minsk integrates (Linux boot scripts → Java app → browser), with GUI from [[wiki/people/ihar-hrybko.md|Igor Hrybko]] + Anton; Pavel needs ~1 week after the PCB. Genadi does housing/stand/lazy-susan via rapid prototypes (2–3 pieces, housing mostly done in July). September-1 is judged a realistic trial date. [[raw/Flex-POS Implementation Planning.md]]
- **Release cadence & the June-1-2026 cliff.** Sept-1 (non-market beta) → **Sept-15 / Sept-30** two-week continuous integration (each more substantial) → **Nov-1 critical market release** (if not ~10 installs by November, "dramatically behind"; no earlier ideal date is realistic) → **Jan-1**: administrative/housekeeping features + **full TITO validation for old and new machines** ("if we don't have it by January 1st people will doubt our capability"). The **June-1-2026 regulator deadline** will trigger a last-moment rush of devices and "some panic," so the hardware must be **future-proofed with expansion provisions** even where the software is minimal. Operator-card provisioning, the lease/kill-switch, and QR-scanner operation are **non-MVP** for Sept-1 (scanner fitted, not operational). [[raw/Flex-POS Implementation Planning.md]]
- **Card-reader max configuration = up to three readers.** Swipe reader **always installed** (needed to issue plastic cards); + optional **full-insert operator/privilege card** reader (could share the swipe channel); + possible future **Incom contactless/chip** reader (PCI-compliance, future reloadable cards). Genadi favours two housings over one 3-part housing; the operator-card-vs-PIN debate recurred (a PIN is needed regardless for the washroom-break lock; the operator card is "more elegant," Val likes it) — resolve by provisioning the housing now. **Product-team cadence:** Mon/Wed/Fri, under 45 min (Nathan/Pavel core, Juan + Genadi added, Val optional); Belarus holiday shortens the week of July-4; Hrybko presents first UX **Wednesday July-2**. [[raw/Flex-POS Implementation Planning.md]]

## Initiative description (spec doc, era ~May 2025)

A standalone **initiative-description spec doc** (not a meeting) summarises FlexPay as JVL's payment/redemption automation for the **GA-COAM** market — hardware (kiosks) + software to comply with Georgia gaming rules. Most of its content is already captured above; the durable net-new specifics it adds, dated by its own "Current Status (as of May 2025)" line: [[raw/flex-pay_initiative_description.md]]

- **Three implementation/business-model options** for how a card converts to cash: (1) **clerk-assisted** (players ask a clerk to swipe for cash); (2) **merchant acquirer** (install card readers with traditional payment processing, **~3% fees**); (3) **barcode solution** — direct processing with **Incomm** using barcodes, the **preferred** option (lower fees). [[raw/flex-pay_initiative_description.md]]
- **Regulatory/compliance specifics:** SAS-event **51 and 52** support for hand-pay conditions; **MCC-code classification as "skill-based games"** for payment processing; **SAS 6.03** protocol compliance for GA-COAM; WSS network protocol; barcode scanning for card activation. Payment flow: win → ticket → redeem at Flex-Pay kiosk → prepaid MasterCard loaded → card usable at other COAM machines (via Flex-Port readers), for lottery tickets, or general MasterCard retail. [[raw/flex-pay_initiative_description.md]]

## History — why FlexPay started (2023–2024 origin)

A research-synthesis document (an AI narrative built over JVL transcripts, dated to the May-27-2025 staging window — not itself a meeting transcript; treat its figures as second-hand summaries of 2023–2024 discussions) captures the origin of the whole FlexPay / GA-COAM initiative. [[raw/why_flex-pay_started-may-27-2025.md]]

- **The trigger (2023–2024):** the **Georgia Lottery approved gift cards as a legal redemption method** for COAM (Coin Operated Amusement Machine) winnings — an opportunity and a problem at once. [[raw/why_flex-pay_started-may-27-2025.md]]
- **The manual-process problem:** a player wins, gets a ticket, and a store **clerk manually scans it and loads the value onto a GLC (Georgia Lottery Corporation) gift card** — labor-intensive, error-prone, high-friction, and dependent on clerk availability/willingness ("the person behind the counter is not really motivated to give the card"). [[raw/why_flex-pay_started-may-27-2025.md]]
- **The money-leakage problem:** once winnings are on a MasterCard-branded gift card, players spend the money elsewhere, so "money was leaving the gaming ecosystem." The circular-economy goal was to let players use gift-card winnings to **play more games** — the core case: "a player wins $100 at a convenience store, loads it onto a MasterCard, and then cannot use that $100 to play at the COAM gaming machines." An April-2025 meeting framed it as merchants worried about "a loss of in-store spend." [[raw/why_flex-pay_started-may-27-2025.md]]
- **Market drivers:** the legislative opening (gift cards "almost like cash" but trackable/compliant); competitive advantage — **United Gaming, representing ~25% of Georgia's ~24,000 machines**, wanted to differentiate and be first to an automated solution ("we'll end up buying more JVL machines because that's what the players will prefer"); and operational efficiency (less cash handling, less clerk intervention, better tracking). [[raw/why_flex-pay_started-may-27-2025.md]]
- **Val's 2023 financial math (business economics):** machines generating **$3,000/day at 90% RTP**; even at **20% gift-card adoption** that's **$540/machine/day** through cards; at **3% processing fees** = **$16/day/machine**; for **3,000 machines** = **$48,000/day** in processing costs — the prize being to keep that money in-house as recurring transaction revenue. [[raw/why_flex-pay_started-may-27-2025.md]]
- **Evolution of the solution:** from initial 2023 concepts — plastic card readers → paper tickets (TITO-style) → QR codes / optical readers → eventually a comprehensive **kiosk + reader** solution. [[raw/why_flex-pay_started-may-27-2025.md]]

## July 1–2 2025 — InComm in-person session: the product array named, the Redemption Center, the mini-ATM

At the two-day InComm strategy meeting in Toronto ([[wiki/meetings/2025-07-01-incomm-product-strategy-toronto.md]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] + team with InComm's [[wiki/people/john-houseal.md|John Houseal]]) Val laid out JVL's full payout-device array and John named the mini-ATM the **"Redemption Center."** InComm-side facts are on [[wiki/organizations/incom.md]]; the device-catalog content: [[raw/incomm_product_discussion-july-1-2025-1.md]]

- **The product array (Val's pitch), to reach market by June 2026.** JVL wants to "wedge itself between InComm and the markets" as validation conduit + equipment supplier, earning ~$1/transaction recurring. Devices: (1) **POS** — wedges between the Mother-Goose board and the cage's dot-matrix (RS-485) printer; on cash-out the screen shows the win, cashier presses **"redeem to card,"** POS validates via InComm and pairs a scanned/swiped card — the minimum path to June-1 legislative compliance. (2) **Tower** — a slim PC-sized tower with an isolated card dispenser + player-facing touchscreen (protected dispensing, no cashier handling the card). (3) **SMIB** — for two-SAS machines, "a $10 device… you could practically give it away." (4) **conversion kit / FlexPort** for one-SAS machines (barcode reader, touchscreen, bill-acceptor + printer connectors, WSS output). (5) **T2 (Tito) server** — validation, can be cloud (risk: cloud down = all down). (6) **FlexPay** — the elaborate tower (full touchscreen, scanner, multiple bill acceptors + dispensers + printer; "Serge" demoed it downstairs). [[raw/incomm_product_discussion-july-1-2025-1.md]] [[raw/incomm_product_discussion-july-2-2025-6.md]] (The card-dispenser cost was discussed but the figure is garbled in the transcript — "our cost is under $500,000" — recorded as unclear, not asserted.) [[raw/incomm_product_discussion-july-2-2025-6.md]]
- **Two agreements per master.** JVL signs an **equipment agreement with the master** and a **processing agreement with the location** (delegated to the master, who tells the location "you're covered, sign here"). Which device/credential submits the activation determines who gets paid (see [[wiki/organizations/incom.md]]). [[raw/incomm_product_discussion-july-2-2025-5.md]]
- **The bill-recycler mini-ATM (Val's key idea) → "Redemption Center."** Val proposed mounting a **bill-to-bill recycler inside the gaming machine** (or in a tower between two machines) so it acts as a **standalone mini-ATM**: bills taken in during play refill the recycling cassette, and small wins are paid out in cash from it — it **never needs refilling** because payouts are always less than revenue (jackpots are never paid in cash). Only JVL machines with the recycler would then be the cash source, so they'd "prevail." Compliance nuance: the player must still be **sold an instrument** (the 353/SkillPay card) that is then converted to cash — auto-converting the load straight to cash "would be illegal." John named it the **Redemption Center** (don't call it an ATM — regulatory optics). The closest existing hardware shown was the **Hyosung Pivot / C-store-of-the-future** unit (~$8,500). Extends the [[#may-12-2025--flexpay-decomposed-as-a-capability-product-discovery-worked-example|bill-recycler / "Cash Code" capability]] and the [[wiki/organizations/jcm.md|JCM recycler]] evaluation. [[raw/incomm_product_discussion-july-1-2025-4.md]] [[raw/incomm_product_discussion-july-2-2025-7.md]]
- **Monthly-fee-per-device business model.** Val's aim is to charge masters a **monthly fee per device**; John floated ~$200/month, Val thought that high. The devices outlive an EGM (embedded, ~5–10 years vs the machine's 3–5), so a rental/recurring model fits. Beyond COAM, the Redemption Center can earn from **non-gaming cash-in services** (top-ups, bill-pay, deposits to Cash App/Chime/Capital One) — detail on [[wiki/organizations/incom.md]]. [[raw/incomm_product_discussion-july-2-2025-7.md]]
- **Naming still fluid: Tower vs Pay vs Redemption Center.** Val: **Pay** = the elaborate product they have today (shown downstairs); **Tower** = a smaller version that scans a barcode and auto-dispenses a card without an attendant; **Redemption Center** = the Tower extended with the bill recycler. "There will be a fusion between Tower, Pay, and the Redemption Center… the vision will get defined as we go along." (A larger-screen **FlexPay V43** was also flagged as needed.) [[raw/incomm_product_discussion-july-2-2025-9.md]] [[raw/incomm_product_discussion-july-2-2025-6.md]]

## July 10 2025 — "Defining product capabilities": Tower vs Redemption Center, outsource the processing module, cloud-processing

A product-capability working session ([[wiki/meetings/2025-07-10-defining-product-capabilities.md]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan Carver]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]]) reworking Val's "product bank." [[raw/Defining Product Capabilities.md]]

- **Flex Tower split from the Flex Redemption Center (the "mini ATM").** Val deliberately separated the two (mergeable later): **Flex Tower** = a very simple device — customer-facing screen + a card-dispensing mechanism only; **Flex Redemption Center** = houses a **bill recycler**. By the end of the meeting the split hardened by **form-fit, not aesthetics**: the **Tower shrinks** to a desktop/counter card-dispenser (all plastic, no security "because there's no value," elegant, sits on a pedestal/counter, screen for validation, easy card refill, no front-service); the **Redemption Center expands** (out of the attendant's sight so it needs heavy **vandal-proofing** — different steel gauge, different lock, end-switches signalling forced entry — front-serviceable, ≤**8–9″ wide**). Juan's practicality note: there is rarely space *between* machines, but always room beside them. [[raw/Defining Product Capabilities.md]]
- **Outsource the transaction-processing module (the meeting's headline).** Val: designing the back-office **transaction-processing / validation** system is far more sophisticated and risky than he had appreciated — "potentially **millions of dollars per hour of fraud**" — and he "question[s] JVL's capability in designing such a product in Minsk." SAS and TITO he has "**zero concern**" about; it is the **banking/transactional** part (instant account validation, swiping the operator's account, **instant automated reconciliation** with no manual reconciliation) that JVL lacks the industry expertise for. Direction: frame **"transaction processing"** as a **separate, self-standing application/module** (independent of the devices), and **subcontract it to a specialist processing company** — subscribe to an existing service now to "learn the ropes," then maybe build in-house ~a year later or hire a specialist to build it with the internal team. Val: "I am not willing to sacrifice security. This is paramount." The **Flex Redemption Center amplifies** the risk because it dispenses **live cash** — the transaction is **irreversible**. [[raw/Defining Product Capabilities.md]]
- **Candidate processors = the InComm-broker ecosystem.** Val recalled John (InComm) highlighting **RWJ, "Lunix Telecom," and "Inlaw" (spelled with a Y)** as companies that specialise in processing transactions, one of which offers its processing to third parties at a fee. (These are the **RWJ / LUNIX / Yinlo** Georgia brokers already documented on [[wiki/organizations/incom.md]] from the Jul-1/2 InComm meeting — "in law with a Y" = **Yinlo**; Juan flagged Lunix's reputation for bad customer service.) Val declined to approach the mainstream casino-industry processors directly — they are "aggressive" and he does not want to "create a casino-born competitor" by revealing JVL's plans; better to work through the InComm-referred processors who already know InComm. [[raw/Defining Product Capabilities.md]]
- **Cloud-processing — a "dramatic change" in architecture (InComm's recommendation).** Nathan relayed InComm's recommendation (from the API docs / prior-day call): do the processing **NOT on-site** but on JVL's **offsite/cloud server**, because InComm **disables by account, not by site-ID** — disabling an account would kill **every storefront** of that customer, and InComm cannot pinpoint-disable an individual Flex POS. So JVL's **cloud server does the actual gift-card activation as a pass-through** (device → JVL cloud → InComm), giving JVL precise control over the pre-authorize → transfer-money → validate → activate sequence, and meaning the field devices carry **no InComm knowledge** (nothing to steal if compromised). Pavel: this means reworking *all* the issuance communication to **proxy through JVL's server** instead of talking to InComm directly — "**lots of rework**," and probably lets JVL drop the on-site server entirely in favour of the cloud. Val: "it's the right approach." Details on [[wiki/organizations/incom.md]]. [[raw/Defining Product Capabilities.md]]
- **SMIB purpose corrected: two SAS ports, not one; FlexPort = "expanded SMIB."** Val's product doc #5 had said SMIB's purpose is "enable machines with only **one** SAS port to connect" — corrected on the call to **two SAS ports** (one port is held by the GLC; the second chains to the server). Val: "Sorry that I made a mistake… I do blame AI for it." The **revised FlexPort board already includes the second SAS connection** but the software is "not scheduled to support this port" yet → **expand the FlexPort software to support the second SAS**. **SMIB** has **no display** (hidden inside the machine, "small box," SAS-connected); **FlexPort** has a display (creates the virtual wallet / player interaction) and **can also be used on a dual-SAS machine** — "port is expanded SMIP." SMIB and FlexPort use **different CPUs/microcontrollers** (same architecture family; modules reusable but "absolutely different software," not interchangeable — Pavel to confirm with Alekseev/Evgenia). Val wants SMIB **extremely inexpensive** — "it has to be a **single digit**… like $100? No — a single-digit thing," i.e. "**two coffees or three tea cups**" (Juan: "sounds like 2 resistors and a wire"). [[raw/Defining Product Capabilities.md]]
- **Product taxonomy.** "**Flex Server**" = the JVL **control server** (the cloud server Flex POS communicates with; also the CMS-like server that manages device leases/setup) — an **onsite/offsite naming convention** is still needed; **firmware updates** can come from a *different* server than the transaction/cloud server. "**Skill pay card**" stands as its own product nomination. **Personas** are already defined in the **GitHub knowledge base** under "personas." The former Flex pay **kiosk** has "morphed into the redemption center." [[raw/Defining Product Capabilities.md]]
- **Recycler / dispenser engineering.** ARM-based board (once the platform is validated); a **ping / keep-alive / watchdog** between all devices (restart on no-reply; the local server hanging is "a major alarm"); Val floated **server redundancy** across the CPUs. The bill recycler uses **4 drums/cassettes** nominated **20/20/20/10** (main payout in 20s; can't exceed 4 denominations); **jackpots always go to the card, never cash**. The recycler mounts **above** the L-shaped card dispenser (skinny 1000-bill cassette at the bottom). Keep a **magnetic-stripe reader + optical scanner + light controller on both** devices. **The bill acceptor must be removed from the machine** — all cash circulation initiates at the Redemption Center so it accumulates the **float** (like the old coin hoppers that were never emptied). Historical: the recycler began as JVL's "**Bill Hopper**," then adopted the term "recycling cassette" from a dying **Japanese patent by a company called Maka**. The competitor handheld is the **PAX ("packs")** — "a cheap Android phone with a swiper," ~**$300**, Wi-Fi + manual entry — which JVL's wired device beats on security. [[raw/Defining Product Capabilities.md]]
- **Undertakings.** **Juan** — obtain (a) the transactional agreement between a clearing-house/broker and a location and (b) the ATM-provider↔location agreement plus the casino-equivalent supplier contracts he has on file (via Felipe / a trusted master / John). **Nathan** — speak to John about the processing module and start the **block-diagram architecture** with **Pavel** for Val's review on his return. **Genadi** — sketch the two devices (spatial planning), keeping the magnetic-stripe reader + optical sensor + light on both. [[raw/Defining Product Capabilities.md]]

## July 10–11 2025 — FlexPort embedded-dev updates: the e-wallet ("WOLOT") architecture wraps, pivot to TITO

Two distinct Russian FlexPort embedded-dev status sessions in the same mid-July window (the Minsk embedded team: [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Evgeniy Alekseev]], [[wiki/people/eugine-yavorski.md|Eugine Yavorski]], [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dzmitry Chudzinau/Dmitri Chudinov]], with [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]], [[wiki/people/alexei-khamin.md|Alexey Khamin]], [[wiki/people/vladislav-gurin.md|Vladislav Gurin]] and [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]]). Both are **undated** — dates inferred (see stubs), ordering QUEUED (Tier-B). The **board-redesign / bill-validator session** ([[wiki/meetings/2025-07-10-flexport-embedded-update-boards-tito.md]]) likely precedes the **e-wallet-wrap session** ([[wiki/meetings/2025-07-11-flexport-embedded-update-ewallet-tito.md]], anchored to the Jul-12 status doc + Val's Jul-12–22 vacation). ("**и volot / WOLOT**" is the transcription of the **e-wallet** architecture.)

- **The e-wallet architecture is ~complete; team pivots to TITO.** The Jul-11 file's status summary puts the **e-wallet architecture at 85–90% complete**. Alekseev reported his tasks on "the architecture we call WOLOT" **finished** — he added a **real-time-clock log file** and a statistics structure, would test over the weekend and hand it to Minsk for testing. Val's read: e-wallet, WOLOT and the SMIB board are reaching a point where they can be "**put on the shelf**" and the team **switches to the TITO architecture** — though full end-to-end testing "may still take ~2 weeks." [[raw/Flex-port_update.md]]
- **FlexPort board correction for production.** Alekseev is correcting the **full ("big") FlexPort** boards — relocating connectors and adjusting mounting holes "so everything is convenient to assemble"; confirmed the FlexPort board **already has two COM/SAS ports** (not yet implemented in software), and the group needs a FlexPort variant that also works in **TITO** mode. In the (garbled) board session FlexPort is described as **3 boards**; the redesign fixes LCD-monitor connector placement and configuration settings ("not errors — settings"), and SMIB has a different board count. [[raw/Flex-port_update.md]] [[raw/Flex-Port_Update.md]]
- **The log-file / FAT-filename detail.** The USB stick that collects machine logs must be reusable for FlexPort logs; the FlexPort copy-name is too long for **FAT16/FAT32** short filenames, which show a **tilde (~)** to signal a truncated long name — so Alekseev must drop one more character to land on an **8-character (8.3) name**, which he verified works. What to log (board session): connection loss/restore, **cash-out**, per-denomination **bill counters**, **bill-rejection frequency**, PIN-entry switch — with Irina asking how much data to store and for how long. [[raw/Flex-port_update.md]] [[raw/Flex-Port_Update.md]]
- **Bill-validator (BDC) work still outstanding.** Genadi flagged the **bill-acceptor/BDC** support as must-finish **before** the hardware is upgraded (a hardware change may follow). Today only the **WJM** validator is supported; support for a special **"Navi"/NMI-Advance** validator is prepared but **untested**. The board session adds that the **BDC protocol still needs implementing (~2 weeks)** and debates a physical-protocol-select **jumper ("перемычка")** — **Irina dislikes the jumper** (fear of mis-setting); the group confirmed a wrong jumper **won't burn the board**, it just won't communicate (recoverable by toggling). Val: don't build the validator protocol under **MIP** now — it isn't the priority. [[raw/Flex-port_update.md]] [[raw/Flex-Port_Update.md]]
- **A machine that won't initialise.** One vendor's machine doesn't even **initialise** when a FlexPort is inserted — needs machine-side initialisation investigation; put on the agenda. [[raw/Flex-port_update.md]]
- **Cash-out is blocked on Juan.** Yavorski spent two weeks on test feedback (sent 2–3 updates, ~5 versions); base e-wallet functionality works **except cash-out**, which waits on **Juan's answer** to a detailed **6-point email** [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina]] sent Juan and **which is still unanswered** — Val undertook to chase it. (The board session shows the team still **locating** that email, corroborating it precedes the Jul-11 session.) Yavorski also asked Chudinov for a small **8-kilobyte** test routine to verify a memory-block load over the FlexPay↔FlexPort link (for the "Super Mario" firmware-download update). [[raw/Flex-port_update.md]] [[raw/Flex-Port_Update.md]]
- **TITO scope — don't build for single-SAS machines.** On whether FlexPort should support the **TITO** configuration on a machine with only **one COM/SAS port** (the legacy Georgia / Mother-Goose case), Val's view: such machines are dying out — "it will die its own death" — so it isn't worth building TITO support just for them (that is what the second SAS port / SMIB is for). The transactional/TITO framing is being worked with [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan Carver]]. SMIB-board **hardware is ready** ("по железу можно уже" — hardware green-light), ~1–2 days more to fully green-light. [[raw/Flex-Port_Update.md]] [[raw/Flex-port_update.md]]

## ~July 15 2025 — POS "harbor"/hardware-platform cost debate (alignment-planning catch-up)

On the [[wiki/meetings/2025-07-15-alignment-planning-catch-up.md|mid-July alignment-planning catch-up]] (Val / [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]] / [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]]), Val pressed to **pin the "harbor" (hardware platform) for at least the POS** soon — right after the architecture is finalized (~a week out) — treating **cost + reliability, not universality**, as the goal because the POS "станет камнем соприкосновения для всех локейшн" (the entry product at every location). [[raw/alignment-planning-catch-up.md]]

- **Flex-Port processor cost question to [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]].** With new gerber files going out for the Flex-Port boards, Val asked whether the processor + peripherals were **optimally (cheaply) chosen**, since this is the last moment to fold in changes. He felt he **couldn't get the cost concern across** to Alekseev, who seemed not to be thinking in that direction. Pavel's rule of thumb: at **~30¢** difference take the more powerful chip; at **$3–$10** it's worth optimizing. [[raw/alignment-planning-catch-up.md]]
- **The POS is a "dumb" device.** It makes no decisions itself — it talks to a server that does validation and tells it what to do. Which server depends on the topology: **Mother-Goose** variant → POS can be self-sufficient/"smarter", no local server; **TITO** variant → a **local server is required** (cloud alone won't work). This is the same fork later settled in the July-23→31 sessions. [[raw/alignment-planning-catch-up.md]]
- **Board-cost ballpark & the "fuss over $20?" question.** Rough figures floated: an **ARM/"Sanpin" board ≈ $50**, another **≈ $60**, the **existing/"Orlov" platform ≈ $80** — Val asked whether it's worth "dуrить себе голову" over a $20 spread. Pavel: for now it's simpler to **keep development in-house** (JVL has the developers); optimize later. A **three-way** hardware fork exists (buy a ready board / solder a module onto JVL's own board / use the current platform), the choice driven by **production volume**, and the hardest thing to estimate is **software dev + ongoing support** effort, not the silicon. [[raw/alignment-planning-catch-up.md]]
- **Linux vs embedded for internet security.** For an internet-facing device, Linux has ready libraries (standard **SSL** key handling, **NTP** time-sync); an **embedded** build would have to implement secure internet transitions from scratch — Pavel flagged **Alekseev (Evgeny)** would have "gораздо больше поработать" to make the secure-internet transition on embedded than on Linux. (Foreshadows the Jul-31 **Linux / MySQL / Java / React** tech-stack pick below.) [[raw/alignment-planning-catch-up.md]]

## July 9–Aug 1 2025 — Flex-POS iteration cluster (agile "iteration" cadence)

The Flex-POS build ran on a Mon/Wed/Fri "iteration" cadence through July. Sequence: **Jul 9 sync → Jul 14 planning → Jul 23 sync (architecture push) → Jul 25 review (whiteboard) → Jul 29 e-wallet deep-dive → Jul 31 continuation (message-queue / Tito / tech-stack) → ~Aug 1 weekly review.**

- **Jul 9 mid-iteration sync** ([[wiki/meetings/2025-07-09-flex-pos-mid-iteration-sync.md]]): FTP-vs-serial debate on capturing the [[wiki/organizations/rks.md|RKS Mother Goose]] ticket. RKS's new **FTP-upload board** ($466, one per location) would replace the field board; Val's counter — if a board must be replaced anyway, replace it with JVL's own **SMIB** (a sub-$10 board they could give away) and sell the operator a JVL server, rather than promote RKS hardware. Pavel found the ticket **confidence number** decomposes as: first ~15 digits = amount, then a 9–10-digit field ≈ a hash/"cache number" + 2 bytes of ticket value; the **2 mystery bytes** are what JVL must learn to fully validate a ticket against fraud. RKS likely never shares the RS-485 protocol (guards its board), so JVL targets the **RS-232 printer protocol** and is confident Evgeny/Yavorski can reverse-engineer it. Val's liability frame for operators: two risk profiles — either JVL **swipes the operator's account within ~5 minutes** of a transaction (operator invests nothing), or the operator installs a JVL server and gets 2 working days to settle. Nathan owns the Jim/RKS interaction. Devs already began a template back-end app + POS screen-layout work; Val told Pavel to **rein them back**, finish [[wiki/people/dmitri-kovalchuk.md|Dima]]'s Tableau integration first, and nail products → capabilities → personas → use-cases → requirements → architecture in that order. Val's vacation **July 12–22**. [[raw/Flex-POS Mid-Iteration Sync.md]]
- **Jul 14 iteration planning** ([[wiki/meetings/2025-07-14-flex-pos-iteration-planning.md]]): Jim confirmed the confidence number is a **hash of the entire ticket data** he can share, and print signals are **all ASCII** (printer type shouldn't matter). Genadi's read: from a hardware view the POS is now mostly defined. Green-lit Pavel's **spike** — connect a real printer, dump all ticket data (read-only; no reprint-from-POS at this stage; the printer stays driven by Mother Goose so no RS-232 multi-master needed). Requirements **FR 1.1** (intercept print data) + **F 1.2** (store complete ticket metadata) move Confluence→Jira; a third item shifts from "generate integrity hash" to **regenerating/verifying the confidence number** to compare. **GitHub becomes the source-of-truth** for requirement docs (per Val's push off Atlassian); Nathan builds a simple `architecture`/`requirements` folder tree in the Flex-POS repo. [[raw/Flex-POS Iteration Planning 2025-07-14.md]]
- **Jul 23 mid-iteration sync** ([[wiki/meetings/2025-07-23-flex-pos-mid-iteration-sync.md]]): Val, back from vacation, refocused the group on the **overall architecture of the "transactional devices"** (his coined term — POS + port + server + Redemption Center as one array), not just POS. Fraud/counterfeit protection is **priority #1**; functionality flows from there. Pavel made a **Mother Goose emulator** (via AI, ~half a day) to generate test tickets, and proposed connecting **in parallel** (one serial cable, ground + data) so Mother Goose + printer keep working if the POS is powered off. Val's architectural concern: he leans toward the POS **wedged in the centre** so that if the POS is out of service everything stops (fraud control), and pressed the **hot-swappable / self-configuring replacement device** requirement (devices should be replaceable, not fixable; a swapped-in unit must auto-configure from a mini-server/cloud, not a phone call). Agreed: a **ping between POS and cloud**; if lost, POS **self-recovers first**, then reports if it can't (Nathan's threshold ~5 min). MVP staging idea (Pavel): stage-1 local redemption server in the POS by ~Sept 1, stage-2 move transactions to cloud by ~October. SMIB-giveaway economics restated: EasyLink ≈ **$600**/machine × ~**2,000 machines/yr** ≈ **$1.2M** the market spends — JVL's SMIB could be free with a POS subscription. **Note: on June 1 next year, all winnings must go to gift cards.** Scheduled a **2-hour architecture deep-dive Tue Jul 29, 8–10 am**; Friday repurposed as the pre-layout call. [[raw/Flex-POS_Mid-Iteration_Sync-2025-07-23.md]]
- **Jul 25 iteration review — the transactional-architecture whiteboard** ([[wiki/meetings/2025-07-25-flex-pos-iteration-review.md]]): Val drove Juan (in Visio) through four device **topographies**: (1) POS-only wedged Mother-Goose↔printer, JVL cloud control server holds POS settings for hot-swap; (2) POS + **countertop card-dispensing unit** (touchscreen + optical scanner + dispenser in a plastic case, ≤170 cards, refilled ~150 at a time; attendant never touches the card); (3) **TITO** setup — fledgling/Mother Goose removed, machines + Flexport/SMIB + local server on a router; (4) **mini-ATM / Redemption Center** with a bill recycler between machines. **Rulings:** the **"tower" concept is dead** — it split into the countertop dispenser and the mini-ATM/Redemption Center; **Flexport can fully act as a SMIB** (no third product); the **Flexpay kiosk has reached end of life** except as the e-wallet product (e-wallet is a separate business architecture, MVP done, not killed). New-ARM-board limit surfaced: it exposes only **4 lines (power/ground/TX/RX)**, so a printer-less mode needs 2 extra serial lines / a fuller COM port — a "small hurdle," solvable on JVL's own board. Val: no Visio/extra tools — use **Confluence whiteboards + GitHub (Mermaid)**. Undertaking: Juan to brainstorm the mini-ATM use case (printer-vs-bill-acceptor player confusion). [[raw/Flex-POS_Iteration_Review-2025-07-25.md]]
- **Jul 29 transactional-architecture deep-dive — the e-wallet architecture** ([[wiki/meetings/2025-07-29-transactional-architecture-ewallet-deep-dive.md]]; Val, Nathan, Juan, Pavel, Genadi): the 2-hour deep-dive scheduled on Jul-23. Nathan whiteboarded the **e-wallet architecture** with the **redemption kiosk as the location hub**. The kiosk **dispenses cash + gift cards, accepts bills, interacts with the e-wallet, dispenses an e-wallet ticket, and scans it**; administrative/config access is by **QR scanner or a mobile app** (no special-access ticket), and it reports events to the **cloud config server**. [[raw/Transactional-Architecture-Discussion-2025-07-29.md]] The **e-wallet is bound to the kiosk/location** — not portable across locations, because cross-location financial reconciliation "would become a mess." A wallet can be **created only at the kiosk**: insert cash → "generate e-wallet" → enter + repeat a **PIN** → prints an **e-wallet ticket with a barcode**; the **PIN is bound to that ticket's barcode**, lose the ticket and the cash is lost; it can't be created from a Flexport. [[raw/Transactional-Architecture-Discussion-2025-07-29.md]] The kiosk **maintains internal state** (physical cash balance + a players' e-wallet database) and **syncs to the JVL control server (cloud)**; Flexports (each inside a machine, machine wired to a router) connect to the kiosk. Machines with **one or two SAS ports** are both supported (same Flexport, different software for SAS vs pulses). [[raw/Transactional-Architecture-Discussion-2025-07-29.md]] A **Flex POS** sits in the cage as backup (scan ticket → create gift card), with a **"manual gift card swipe"** fallback where a location has no tabletop dispenser; a router + likely a **network switch** wire it all up. Gift cards still depend on **Incom + an ACH transfer** (Georgia: the location transfers to JVL when a gift card is redeemed). [[raw/Transactional-Architecture-Discussion-2025-07-29.md]] Device debate: Genadi floated a **simplified Flexport** for no-bill-validator machines, but PIN entry needs a **GUI/touchscreen/video**, so the group leaned to **one robust Flexport across everything** (cost difference small; **deferred** — "talk about it later"). Security: **SSL / WSS (Web Socket Secure)** to the control server, WSS from Flexports to the kiosk; boards are **STM**. [[raw/Transactional-Architecture-Discussion-2025-07-29.md]] **Processing application / master:** the ticket-processing app can run on a **kiosk, the Flex POS, or a separate location server**; because one processor handles all tickets **sequentially**, a **copied ticket scanned at two kiosks can't double-redeem** (the second read returns $0). Val pushed **database clustering/replication** for redundancy — the server is the **single point of failure for the whole location** — and wants it a **design requirement from the start** (a cluster DB is a "known entity" that could be bought or subcontracted as a module). [[raw/Transactional-Architecture-Discussion-2025-07-29.md]] **Risk scenarios:** copied-ticket double-scan (mitigation = the sequential single processor); a **fraudulent gift card at the POS** (mitigation = must scan the e-wallet ticket, **no manual entry**); **internet down** (the local "bank" keeps working except Incom card redemption — hold the ticket; switch to an **LTE/3G modem**; recommend a **cellular backup** in the sales pitch); **wire-tap/network attack** (register **MAC addresses** / port security at the switch — in tension with hot-swap). [[raw/Transactional-Architecture-Discussion-2025-07-29.md]] **Dispute-resolution report:** any scanner-equipped device should return a short "**not valid**" or an **extended report** (when the ticket was generated / played / cashed) — an **MVP for the database designers**. Add a **magnetic swiper to the redemption kiosk** to verify a gift-card balance (and later convert card→e-wallet); **Juan** to follow up with Incom on swipe-to-verify. Incom onboarding needs an **Incom form per location** with terminal-ID numbers and can take **up to 36 hours** to authorize a location. [[raw/Transactional-Architecture-Discussion-2025-07-29.md]] **Redemption-center physical design:** ~**8-inch-wide**, **front-loading / front-servicing** unit that **rolls out** (front-heavy — needs a front support/wheel), **bolted to the floor** or on a **weighted pedestal**; placed **between machines** or (per Juan, more realistically) at the end of a row / beside an ATM; two pedestal shapes (machine-profile vs large weighted foot); Genadi floated putting a **recycler inside the existing Flexpay** for a standalone variant. Logistics: Val **cancelled the Jul-30 mid-iteration sync** and booked **Thursday Jul-31, 2 hours** for the continuation. [[raw/Transactional-Architecture-Discussion-2025-07-29.md]]
- **Jul 31 continuation — message-queue replication, the Tito diagram, tech-stack** ([[wiki/meetings/2025-07-31-transactional-architecture-continuation.md]]; Val, Nathan, Juan, Pavel, Genadi): Nathan had added **summary docs to the GitHub repo + a Confluence diagram**. Terminology settled: **single-kiosk (basic)** vs **multiple-kiosk (advanced)** e-wallet config, and "**JVL server**" replaces "location hub." [[raw/transactional_architecture-july-31-2025.md]] **Data-sync approach: event-driven message-queue replication, chosen over database clustering** — Nathan reframed the Jul-29 "clustering" (DB-specific) as **event-driven replication via a message queue** (no cluster needed): a designated kiosk holds the **master queue**, every device (other kiosks, POS, JVL cloud/control server) **subscribes**, each transaction is pushed to all subscribers with **guaranteed delivery** (held until the internet returns), and the master queue is **swappable**. This **supersedes the Jul-29 clustering framing**. (Nathan had Claude generate the trade-off matrix.) [[raw/transactional_architecture-july-31-2025.md]] **Server-as-software = a redundancy requirement:** the location server is an **application, not a physical box**, installable on any hardware (kiosk or POS); **every main JVL product (POS, redemption kiosk) must be built able to host the server application** so a downed device's peer can take over — Val made it a requirement. **Flexports are always slave devices** (can't run a message queue or database) — they talk **directly to a kiosk**, whose storage is their persistence; a **POS-only site needs no message queue** (direct communication; no printer to make an e-wallet ticket anyway). [[raw/transactional_architecture-july-31-2025.md]] **Data retention:** keep local data **≤ ~7 days and overwrite (not wipe)** it, with an **hourly** dump to the cloud — to minimize exposure if a device is stolen; Pavel preferred periodic cloud snapshots + never deleting (fewer write commands), but Val insisted on the weekly overwrite. The **cloud keeps extended data "forever"** for historical reporting/analysis (Juan). [[raw/transactional_architecture-july-31-2025.md]] **Tito diagram** (adapted from the e-wallet one): **no e-wallet, must have a printer, no dispenser**; Tito ticket numbers are generated/validated in the **processing application (Flex POS)**; printing is part of the **SAS protocol**. The consume-the-ticket problem — Tito's premise is to **take possession of / obsolete** the redeemed ticket; a bill-acceptor + stacker is expensive and Val fears the stacker fills and nobody empties it. His alternative: a device that reads the barcode then **cuts/shreds or defaces** the ticket (Pavel's "hot device" blacks out the barcode; a laser/heat-lamp floated; chemical rejected). Resolution: keep bill-acceptor dispensing towers as the **least-favourable** outside option; **Val's undertaking** to ask engineering about a **flat-bed device** that transports → reads → dispenses the ticket **shredded/defaced**, with a garbage can beside it; fold it into the **countertop card-dispenser** if it can't be built. The dispute-resolution report (short "not valid" / extended) was restated as an MVP for the database designers. [[raw/transactional_architecture-july-31-2025.md]] **Tech stack (Pavel), for the on-location devices: Linux OS, MySQL, Java backend, React GUI** — the board "doesn't matter." [[raw/transactional_architecture-july-31-2025.md]] **Operator mobile app:** a basic **location-performance intelligence** app connecting to the cloud, to be shown in **all schematics** even though not MVP (Nathan's undertaking); the cloud **gateway becomes a collection of APIs**. [[raw/transactional_architecture-july-31-2025.md]] **SMIB give-away strategy (Val):** the market is moving toward replacing **Mother Goose** with JVL **SMIB** boards — give the SMIB **free** in exchange for a **3-year JVL POS contract** (a new machine otherwise needs a "fledgling"/interface board at ~**$110**, discounted ~**$80**; JVL's SMIB **$0**); this is a **Mother-Goose retrofit** — neither Tito nor e-wallet. [[raw/transactional_architecture-july-31-2025.md]] **Next steps:** move to **database design + hardware/tech-stack selection**, flagging the **technological debt** in JVL's previous database design; engage the internal database team — **[[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dima (Chudinov)]] + [[wiki/people/sergey-tsymanov.md|Sergey Tsymanov]]** (transcribed "Sergey Smanov"), already reviewing schematics with Pavel. POS: board + major components selected → proceed **spatial then industrial design** ([[wiki/people/igor-starovoytov.md|Igor Starovoytov]] engaged over the weekend). Design fundamentals reaffirmed for every JVL product: **passport, remote initial setup (RIS), lease**. Meeting schedule: Fri + Mon internal engineering; **Wed architecture** (Val/Nathan/Pavel); **Fri POS design review** (Genadi). [[raw/transactional_architecture-july-31-2025.md]]
- **~Aug 1 weekly review** ([[wiki/meetings/2025-08-01-flex-pos-weekly-review.md]]; date inferred, §11): Juan collected ATM kiosk service/master agreements from three large casino manufacturers + is getting a **Georgia ATM contract** via Felipe (all into Confluence); RKS quote for **4 sets** of Mother Goose ships to Toronto. Nathan added **C4-model** architecture docs (context + sequence diagrams) to the Flex-POS repo using **Mermaid** (free, GitHub-rendered) — Val had not heard of C4. Third-party banking integration would add **one more cloud-server API call before the Incom final activation** (verify money taken before activating the card). Val wants **MCP-based flexible querying** (CSV export consumed by Claude) instead of rigid Tableau/Excel reports, so a talented analyst can "freestyle" the data. Fraud architecture: geo-locate equipment (fraud spreads within walking distance, so shut down the surrounding area), standard **alert formats to a distribution group** (e.g. 10 → 100 cash-outs), controlled shutdown from the US while Minsk sleeps. Infra requirements: servers **geographically independent** ("Belarus is now a black swan"), scalable, redundant. **Omni HHR starts Aug 7**; Pavel/Juan/Will to meet next week; [[wiki/people/anton-leshkevich.md|Anton Leshkevich]]'s Omni automated-test framework to extend to the transactional products. Val on vacation "next week"; wants full WVTT recordings. (Both Val and Nathan use Obsidian + Claude Code for personal knowledge bases; Val prefers Claude Desktop — "cloud code has too much power," he might break things.) [[raw/Flex-POS Iteration Review.md]]

## Aug 5 2025 (date inferred) — embedded FlexPort progress review: update mechanism, 3→1 board consolidation, TITO-via-FlexPort, expo prep

The embedded/hardware team's round-table ([[wiki/meetings/2025-08-05-flexport-embedded-progress-review-update-mechanism-boards.md]]; Russian; date inferred — [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/eugine-yavorski.md|Eugine Yavorski]], [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Evgeniy Alekseev]], [[wiki/people/dmitri-chudinov.md|Dmitri Chudinov]] ("Dzmitry Chudzinau"), [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]]). [[raw/Flex-Port progress review.md]]

- **The FlexPort firmware-update mechanism (use-case worked out).** A new firmware version lands first on **FlexPay ("Flexy")**, which downloads it and FlexPort pulls it in **64 MB in 8 KB chunks (≈8,000 chunks)** in the background without disturbing play; FlexPort checks for updates via 3 commands (RS-232/card and internet paths — the Ethernet path talks to a "spool exe" that reports whether an update exists). Yavorski reported completing FlexPort **system-time synchronization** with Alekseev (timestamp obtained at registration; a 2-second connectivity check runs continuously). The debate on **who authorizes the upgrade** resolved to Val's use-case: **don't rely on a human pressing a button** — apply automatically in a **low-activity local-time window (settled on 2–3 AM local; "3–4 AM" also floated)**, add logic to **skip the update if a transaction is unfinished**, and if a unit is powered off during the window it updates on next power-up. The request carries **3 possible answers (yes / no / schedule)**; no answer within the window defaults to yes. Val's principle: don't reinvent the wheel — mirror how phones/cars push updates, not consumer smart-TVs. Longer-term the decision should move to a **server** (and later a mobile app), but stays on FlexPay for now "until we have the server part." A newer firmware supersedes an un-installed buffered one (erase the buffered segment, download the new). USB-stick load remains the emergency fallback (Alekseev: already implemented, works from ART and USB). [[raw/Flex-Port progress review.md]]
- **3-boards → 1-board consolidation (Alekseev + Peari).** The "sandwich" of **three PCBs** is being consolidated to **one board** (~6.5 cm longer, all connectors on it, 8 transitional connectors saved, no separate assembly). Cost: each of the current boards ~**$26** (main board "20–26," at volume, all components); the bare board delta is only ~$1–2, but eliminating **3 separate manufacturing/kitting/assembly/test flows** yields ~**25–30% savings** — from ~**$50 → ~$35 assembled** for the board. Full machine BOM tallied ~**$90** (board $35 + monitor/touchscreen ~$20–22 + scanner-with-interface-board ~$20 + harness ~$10–12). Layout ~**1.5 weeks** to hand to the supplier, +~**2 weeks** to receive → **~Sept 1**. (This is the precursor to the Aug-19 single-board Flex-Port decision below.) [[raw/Flex-Port progress review.md]]
- **TITO-via-FlexPort (SAS).** In **TITO mode** the gaming machine is the initiator: it issues **SAS-protocol** requests over a COM port to FlexPort, which relays a JVL-designed protocol to FlexPay; **FlexPay acts as a local server** (it "thinks the ticket is created," replies) so the machine behaves as if talking to a real system server — **no outside/online server needed** (works on a local network/router). The classic TITO receiver/jackpot tickets weren't implemented yet (classic mode being tested that day). Same update-cycle/communication logic applies to both the **e-wallet** and **TITO** architectures; **SMIB** would be moved onto this mode as the next step. [[raw/Flex-Port progress review.md]]
- **Georgia-expo demo configuration.** JVL will show **both e-wallet and TITO** configs at the show (Val recalled ~10 days out, expo ~Aug 15/20). Setup: **~8 FlexPorts, 2 FlexPays, 8 developer machines** — two "banks" of machines, one per FlexPay/config, each machine with a FlexPort. For the demo a **fake card issue** (no InComm connection) will just dispense a card and report success. Peari confirmed enough FlexPorts on hand in Georgia (can assemble 8). Alekseev noted the TITO config just needs 12 V power fed to the FlexPorts. [[raw/Flex-Port progress review.md]]
- **Flex-POS Linux/ARM board groundwork.** The Flex-POS ("Flex-Pos") runs **Linux on an ARM board** (a different kernel from the x86 boards); Minsk will handle the Linux install — pull the device's **root filesystem**, add JVL's **Java application + GUI**, and compose a new image on a **build / GitHub-Actions server**. Open items flagged to work with Genadi: **GPIO** use (for door/counter/lamp signals, taken directly off the ARM platform pins), whether a **Dallas chip** is needed for a device identifier/cabinet-number registration (maybe an internal identifier suffices), and a small **interface board** on the POS to break out Mother-Goose→printer signals and connections. This work can start once Alekseev frees up (~1.5 weeks). [[raw/Flex-Port progress review.md]]

## Aug 6 2025 — mid-iteration sync (capabilities & data schemas) + the Prize-Card use-case discovery

Two same-day artifacts. **(1) The mid-iteration sync** ([[wiki/meetings/2025-08-06-flex-pos-mid-iteration-sync.md]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan Carver]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]]) reviewed Nathan's GitHub capability dashboard. [[raw/2025-08-06_Flex-POS-Mid-Iteration-Sync.md]]

- **The "FR" (flex-requirement) capability dashboard.** Nathan's GitHub dashboard lists JVL's system capabilities; each requirement is stored in GitHub as the **single source of truth** with a JIRA link that "circles back" (JIRA carries the GitHub info; updates always live in GitHub). Currently **9 capabilities**, in two maturity states Nathan updates manually (helped by Claude): "**in review**" (the first two — iterated and being put into JIRA) and "**identified**" (high-level only). [[raw/2025-08-06_Flex-POS-Mid-Iteration-Sync.md]]
- **Capability #1 = "Mother Goose ticket storage and processing."** Its description: the **Flex-POS replaces the manual human factor** of validating and printing a Mother Goose ticket with **digital validation and activation of a COAM gift card**. The current capability set is **POS-specific and built on the Mother-Goose schema** — Pavel noted the capability list for a Tito/e-wallet POS "will be a little bit different." [[raw/2025-08-06_Flex-POS-Mid-Iteration-Sync.md]]
- **Split the ticket data structures by type.** Because the drive so far has been Flex-POS with Mother Goose, the team will create **separate GitHub folders for the Mother Goose ticket vs the Tito vs the e-wallet** data structures, to show how they differ (Pavel: Tito and e-wallet tickets differ — one carries a value, the other doesn't). Val asked for a generated **"differences / to-do list"** for converting a location from Mother-Goose ticket storage to e-wallet ticket storage. [[raw/2025-08-06_Flex-POS-Mid-Iteration-Sync.md]]
- **Use-case-driven requirement mapping.** Nathan builds requirements by stepping through a well-defined **happy-path use case** (game → cash-out → printed ticket → intercepted player → redeems a gift card), mapping the capability requirements to each step; next use case = **cashier authentication**, then Pavel's device use case. Val asked to add the **service-technician and master-operator personas ahead of the customer** (the customer is already well understood). Val praised the discipline: "probably the closest we came to a perfection so far." [[raw/2025-08-06_Flex-POS-Mid-Iteration-Sync.md]]
- **Everything in GitHub; cadence reset.** Val's "overwhelming preference" is that all iterations (incl. Genadi's hardware requirements, moving off Confluence) live in **GitHub** as the natural place for versioned idea evolution. Cadence restructured: **daily short working meetings** for Nathan/Juan/Pavel; Val + Genadi step out of the working meetings and join only a **weekly Wednesday** review (Val prefers Wednesday over Friday). [[raw/2025-08-06_Flex-POS-Mid-Iteration-Sync.md]]

**(2) The Prize-Card use-case discovery** — a same-day async chat thread ([[raw/Flex-Port-use_case-August-6-2025.md]]) after [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] called [[wiki/people/john-houseal.md|John]] at [[wiki/organizations/incom.md|InComm]] to resolve something that had been bothering him about the architecture. (This is a standalone use-case artifact, **not** a derived summary of the meeting above — it captures a distinct discovery, so it is cited here rather than folded into a stub.)

- **The missed use case (for the July-1-2026 model).** Under Mother Goose: a player wins → approaches the cage → a **cashier operating Flex-POS issues a PRIZE CARD** (a Georgia-Lottery *prize* card, **not** a MasterCard prepaid card). The player can walk away with the Prize Card **or** return to a machine and play more by presenting it — machines must have a **Flex-Port installed** so the player can **scan the Prize Card with the built-in optical scanner**. Val's read: this "**renders TiTo obsolete**" and "kills any need for a TiTo." Open technical question raised: how to communicate the card's value to a machine with a **single SAS port** vs one with **two SAS ports**. [[raw/Flex-Port-use_case-August-6-2025.md]]
- **Card format → the swipe reader is dropped.** John confirmed that **by end of year all Prize Cards will have barcodes** (it is a bar-coded card), so the previously-discussed idea of adding a **magnetic swipe-card reader to Flex-Port** is now deemed unnecessary (Juan: "we did discuss it, but it seems that we do not need it"). [[raw/Flex-Port-use_case-August-6-2025.md]]
- **Reloadability question → e-Wallet preserved.** Val asked whether the Prize Card is **reloadable at the machine** (cash winnings back onto it) — "if the answer is YES, then what becomes of e-Wallet." John replied: "**All Prize Cards are not rechargeable for now**." Treating them as non-rechargeable **preserves e-Wallet as the official cash-out route** when a player moves machine to machine; for e-wallet, JVL can do a **bill-acceptor-emulation transfer** taking money from the card via the cloud InComm processing server. [[raw/Flex-Port-use_case-August-6-2025.md]]
- **Simplified new-machine kit.** Val's aside: on all **new** machine purchases (any manufacturer), a master **no longer needs a Fledgelink board or a Mutha Goose** — all they need is **Flex-Port, Flex-POS, and a Flex-Card-Dispenser**. [[raw/Flex-Port-use_case-August-6-2025.md]]

## Aug 14 2025 — transactional slice of the backend-architecture kickoff

The transactional-products architecture was one thread of the Aug-14 backend-architecture kickoff ([[wiki/meetings/2025-08-14-backend-architecture-cms-transactional-hhr.md]]; the cross-cutting ruling is on [[wiki/decisions/2025-08-14-backend-architecture-block-diagram-first-mvp.md]], the CMS/orchestration side on [[wiki/topics/persona-driven-design-and-orchestration-platform.md]]). Transactional-device points that advance this arc: [[raw/backend_architecture-august-14-2025.md]]

- **Calendar priority reaffirmed.** Transactional products must hit the Georgia market by the **July 1 2026** legislative deadline, beta ~**January 2026** at the Georgia showroom — the *calendar* priority (CMS/HHR are the *architectural* debt behind it). [[raw/backend_architecture-august-14-2025.md]]
- **Three external interactions named.** (1) the **Georgia Lottery / regulatory** SAS-connected system (records the game's cash-out but not the financial gift-card transaction or the $2 service fee); (2) the **payment clearance house** (InComm); (3) the legacy **90%-of-Georgia serial architecture** — an RS-485/RS-232 Mother-Goose board **daisy-chained machine-to-machine**, the final board driving the cage printer — which JVL **wedges into** by intercepting the printer output (machine #, transaction number, dollar amount, date/time) to validate a cash-out, "the most hackable, insecure thing." [[raw/backend_architecture-august-14-2025.md]]
- **Separate payment-processing cloud server (Pavel).** Beyond the orchestration platform, payment products need a **separate cloud server that performs the payment operations** with the third party (InComm or a replacement), living **independently** from the orchestration platform so it can be updated/reinstalled/scaled on its own — "probably two different servers instead of one." [[raw/backend_architecture-august-14-2025.md]]
- **Hot-swap provisioning — Aaron's POS-industry pattern.** Aaron described his prior **Fujitsu Retail Systems + Verifone** POS stack as the model: **cryptographically-signed, fully-replaceable** hardware with an **encrypted-at-rest disk image** that cannot even boot the app until a **key is provisioned from the back office** (serial number ↔ key in a live master database), with **key rotation**, secure-enclave/IP-origin PCI controls, and offline transaction-log replay. Applied to JVL: a replaced POS auto-configures on power-up, but Val added a **provisioning handshake** — when a new POS connects, the operator is asked "we're about to switch your POS, do you allow it?"; Pavel's caveat: **auto-assigning a device to a location isn't reliable without an additional confirmation step**, and it implies a live DB of every POS serial produced. [[raw/backend_architecture-august-14-2025.md]]
- **Business requirements driving the architecture** (restated here): non-technical **hot-swap** replacement; **one-day ACH** swipe of accounts (two-day processor window) with immediate per-transaction reconciliation; **immediate fraud lockout** (Val ignores InComm's fraud capability — "our own money, our own skin in the game"); modern/scalable IT with **no local servers to maintain** where possible; on-location **master/slave** device (server-app on each device, one talks to the InComm payment cloud). [[raw/backend_architecture-august-14-2025.md]]
- **Markets & scope.** Bars/restaurants/gas stations, typically **≤9 machines** by law, transient play, **no player tracking** (only **customer/operator tracking**); the transactional accessory is **loosely regulated** (regulation ends at the skill machine), favouring a **cloud** solution with some on-site processing. [[raw/backend_architecture-august-14-2025.md]]

## Aug 19 2025 — Flex-POS dual-screen dispenser + Flex-Port single-board hardware review

A Val↔[[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]] hardware design one-on-one ([[wiki/meetings/2025-08-19-flex-dispenser-review.md]]; Russian, 4:46pm) advancing the physical Flex-POS dispenser and Flex-Port board. [[raw/flex_dispenser-review.md]]

- **Flex-POS → a single dual-screen dispenser product (design pivot).** Stacking the existing Point-of-Sale (Point XL, an 8-inch screen) on top of the dispenser would give the product **two motherboards and two internet feeds** — "not very correct." Genadi proposes instead **one dispenser cabinet with two screens** (cashier + player), **one motherboard driving both monitors**, a scanner at each; Val prefers this to two separate products ("I hadn't thought in this direction … I like it a lot"). It "fits Georgia's requirements." [[raw/flex_dispenser-review.md]]
- **Form/fit iterations.** Both screens angled symmetrically (a recessed, tidied look, front screen mirroring the back); scanner mounted under each screen; all interconnects hidden inside a **symmetric hexagonal base/"teapot-saddle" plate** with the cable brought out through a **central channel with four 90° exits** so wiring can leave on any side — "nobody can pull it out, you won't even see the cables." Screen width settled toward **~8 inch / ~17 cm** ("give it room to breathe"); **5 inch rejected** as too small; the pricier long screen ($90 vs $20) rejected. [[raw/flex_dispenser-review.md]]
- **Flex-Port on one board.** [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Alekseev]] laid out **Flex-Port on a single PCB** (enlarged ~3.5 inch) replacing the three-board assembly — "one board, no assembly." Plan: Alekseev orders a **minimum sample run (~3, up to 10)** to verify it works, then **finalize**; a minimal plastic **insert/base plate** so operators can mount it. **Minsk doesn't need units** (functionality unchanged). [[raw/flex_dispenser-review.md]]
- **Flex-Port economics (company/vendor figures).** Genadi cited **~30% (minimum) cost saving** from the single board (Val recalled "about $20"): the electronics board ~**$35–38**, plus a **touchscreen ~$18–20**, plus a **scanner-with-board ~$15**, plus an expensive **bill-acceptor interop cable ~$10–12** — one board avoids assembling/testing three boards. [[raw/flex_dispenser-review.md]]
- **Next steps.** Genadi to take exact monitor dimensions from the China supplier, redo the Flex-POS mock-up per Val's notes, then hand it to industrial designer [[wiki/people/igor-starovoytov.md|Igor Starovoytov]]; a follow-up review booked for **Thursday**. [[raw/flex_dispenser-review.md]]

## Aug 20 2025 — CMS/PayFlex block-diagram review + Val's canonical device definitions

The [[wiki/meetings/2025-08-20-cms-payflex-block-diagram.md|Aug-20 block-diagram review]] ([[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]], [[wiki/people/will-jones.md|Will Jones]], [[wiki/people/nathan-carver.md|Nathan]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]], 12:04 PM — session 1 of a three-part CMS block-diagram arc, tracked on [[wiki/topics/persona-driven-design-and-orchestration-platform.md]]) walked Will through the whole PayFlex ecosystem, then Val followed up with **written definitional corrections**. The CMS/cloud direction is on the orchestration-platform page; the device/protocol substance and Val's naming ruling are here. [[raw/CMS- Block Diagram_Arch.md]]

- **In the meeting, the device names were used loosely** (and an AI summary reversed two of them). Juan described FlexPay as "a kiosk which can accept cash, scan a ticket and also dispense a gift card," Flex POS as the small tabletop redemption device, and FlexPort as the player-facing scanner+display "like an iView"; Pavel listed **Flex POS (tabletop small device), Flex Tower (self-dispensing cards unit), Flex Pay (currently implemented device)**, noting each carries smart hardware and can act as a **master device** (processes all ticket types — regular TITO or temporary-wallet — and can combine in any order, e.g. POS+tower, POS+FlexPay). [[raw/CMS- Block Diagram_Arch.md]]

- **Val's canonical definitions (owner correction, folded from his written follow-up).** Val noted the meeting notes/AI summary defined the products incorrectly — **"Flex-Pay and Flex-Tower definitions should be reversed"** — and gave the correct naming convention, now the authoritative version for the wiki: [[raw/val_comments_to_CMS_Block_Diagram_Arc.md]]
  - **Flex-Tower** — small-footprint device for **card dispensing and ticket scanning**.
  - **Flex-POS** — **tabletop** point-of-sale device for **converting tickets to gift cards**.
  - **Flex-Port** — **player-facing** device with scanner and display (like a casino iView).
  - **Flex-Pay** (also *incorrectly* referred to as "**Kiosk**") — **self-dispensing card unit**; functional units were being demoed at the Georgia show that week.
  - **SMIB** — a separate product: an **inexpensive bi-directional converter from SAS to WSS**. [[raw/val_comments_to_CMS_Block_Diagram_Arc.md]] (Reconciles with the earlier "SMIB has no display, hidden inside the machine, SAS-connected" framing on the [[#july-10-2025--defining-product-capabilities-tower-vs-redemption-center-outsource-the-processing-module-cloud-processing|Jul-10 section]].)

- **Mother Goose scheme — Val's corrected definition + where it's heading.** The AI summary had said Mother Goose "prints receipts for manual card creation"; Val's correction: Mother Goose Integration **intercepts legacy pulse-based systems, interprets the value of a win, validates the transaction through InComm, and credits the win onto a prepaid MasterCard** — and JVL is **evolving this scheme to further the transaction to autonomously dispensing an actual MC card**. [[raw/val_comments_to_CMS_Block_Diagram_Arc.md]]

- **Go-to-market priority = Flex-POS + dispenser, with ACH at its core (Val).** Val's stated "first course of business is to release the Flex-POS + dispenser into the market." The most pressing capabilities for that release: (1) **install and automatically configure Flex-POS + dispenser out of the box**; (2) **establish an ACH (automated clearing house) agreement with a location**; (3) **debit the location's account through ACH within 24 hours** per the agreement; (4) **present reconcilable accounting details to locations and master operators**. [[raw/val_comments_to_CMS_Block_Diagram_Arc.md]] (Consistent with the Aug-14 "one-day ACH swipe + immediate reconciliation" business requirement above.)

- **Master-operator legislation applies to slot machines only (Val's correction).** In the meeting Juan explained Georgia's structure — JVL cannot sell directly to a location (e.g. a 711); it sells to a **master operator / master location** who deploys and services the machines, and JVL is deliberately kept blind to installation sites (Intralot reports mask the machine serial with a state-generated ID; only flat-fee sales/lease/long-term, **no revenue sharing** allowed). [[raw/CMS- Block Diagram_Arch.md]] **Val's correction:** the requirement to sell **through master operators is only true for slot machines** — "the transactional equipment is not bound by the same legislation." He also queried the meeting's claim that the "CMS portal needs multi-tenant capabilities for operator self-administration" and asked for elaboration. [[raw/val_comments_to_CMS_Block_Diagram_Arc.md]]

- **Protocols and engineering facts.** Three device-communication models were named (all already documented above): (1) **Mother Goose** pulse interception (pre-SAS machines — intercept bill-acceptor input + hard-meter output pulses; the machine-side serial converter is the "**fledgling**" board); (2) the proprietary **e-wallet** — a temporary, PIN-protected barcode wallet over **secure web socket**, redeemable back to a card; (3) **TITO/SAS** — a Flex SMIB board converts SAS↔Ethernet to the master. **New this session:** Georgia mandated **dual SAS** a couple of years back (newer machines only) — **primary SAS is reserved to Intralot** (the state monitoring system, the only party that can disable a game); JVL uses the **secondary SAS port with slightly limited functionality** (transactions only; **no lock/unlock, no progressives** on third-party games — JVL has its own progressive controller for its own games).
