## Apr 15 2026 — Flex POS Alpha-1 on ARC 35-68; IO-board cost; HAL microservicing; recycler/dispenser Minsk logistics; Flex Pay ICOM refactor

A ~51-minute morning alignment ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-15-flex-pos-alpha-and-omni-2-architecture.md|Flex POS Alpha and OMNI 2 Architecture]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]]) — the device/logistics half is below; the architecture-first OMNI 2 strategy lives on [[wiki/topics/omni-core-advancement-and-game-templating.md]] (Apr 15 2026 section). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]

- **Flex POS Alpha-1 nearly ready on the ARC 35-68 board.** Device-side is "near the finish" — "Lyosha" (a Minsk dev, not force-matched to a page) is installing software; the IO board and the small P-shaped auxiliary IO board are in hand, the harness and the screens are in place. The SDK / development infrastructure is now in JVL's git as **IP 977** — "not 100% complete, but complete enough to use now." Two items outstanding for the Alpha-1 declaration: **speakers are not yet working** (Pavel deferred as non-MVP — the card reader and QR scanner already emit sound, so the kiosk isn't silent; Val wants the speakers resolved in the short term regardless), and the **top light over the swipe reader** has not yet been brought up. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Production security work deferred from Alpha.** For production rollout Pavel flagged an encrypted-bootloader task using the CPU's built-in mechanisms — a multi-week job, owner candidates Pavel or [[wiki/people/aaron.md|Aaron]] (could go to Sasha Beratko but the Linux bootloader would be hard for him). Not needed for a pilot run. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **IO board — push for a Flex-POS-specific redesign instead of reusing the standard board.** Val believes JVL's standard IO board is expensive ("silly money") and argued for a new layout designed specifically for Flex POS, exploiting the modern ease of IO-board layout to cut cost; Pavel agreed this is a discussion for [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]]. (Val's rough alternative for the HAL-abstraction case: take the standard iBoard + a car-key connector, which would "already work.") [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Cellular modem + GPS — HFM-532 filed.** Pavel filed feature **HFM-532 (Wireless Interfaces Support)** today. Val's earlier written spec — a battery-backed cellular+GPS module able to run unpowered for a time to track the unit during transport — is not in the dev infrastructure yet; Pavel treats it as probably a separate IP/project. Val noted turnkey on-board modules (cellular + GPS + orientation sensors in one part) exist to buy off-the-shelf; bring up cellular first, then GPS, then the orientation algorithm. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Flex POS testing is blocked on the UI graphical layer — Anton Seregin must finish this week.** Backend services are deployed and running on AWS; the missing piece is the graphical-UI services. [[wiki/people/anton-seregin.md|Anton Seregin]] starts on those tomorrow (Apr 16). Pavel's plan had UI ready next week / full deploy by end of next week; [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina]] objected forcefully ("we need it now, we cannot test anything, we have a stopper"). Val backed her — no weekends in principle, but Anton will likely be asked to work Saturday so end-to-end testing can start next week. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **HAL → per-device microservices (Step 1 in C).** Today the Hardware Abstraction Layer is a monolith: when one device fails (e.g. the sound card) the whole HAL stack fails, the app crashes, and the machine reboots (black screen at worst). Pavel proposes splitting HAL into independent microservices, one per device class (IO board, printer, bill acceptor, accounter board…), each on its own communication channel — enabling independent reboot, independent update, and independent per-device end-to-end tests (e.g. printer ↔ LGS). **Step 1 is the split, still in C; Step 2 (later) rewrites individual services in Rust as needed** (Val first read it as "rewrite in Rust"; Pavel corrected). Val's mental model: the HAL has two ends — the side talking to peripheral devices (could be assigned to [[wiki/people/anton-leshkevich.md|Lishkevich]]) and the side talking to the data center (the part Pavel wants to modularize). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Dispenser / recycler — the Minsk logistics squeeze.** The **dispenser** is physically en route to Minsk (Genadi confirmed; the Fujitsu unit) — Sasha will pick up dispenser implementation after finishing "Repa" documents, working blind until it arrives (without the device you can only test happy-path, not negative scenarios). The **recycler** reaches Canada within a week but Minsk not for ~a month; Val says they don't have a month, so implementation proceeds remotely — an acknowledged-suboptimal compromise (Sasha can't insert/extract banknotes remotely, so he coordinates verbally with Sergey on-site while watching the debugger). Economics Val laid out: China-built recycler+dispenser samples weigh ~100 kg on a skid; air freight from China at **~$12/kg = ~$1,200 + taxes ≈ $2,000** total. Routing existing Japanese dispensers or Mexican recyclers *through* China is prohibitively expensive because Chinese import duties are non-recoverable on re-export. A Chinese supplier (transcript garble "MI"; notes read **MEI**) had two recycler units in stock and JVL took both; now sold out and won't reorder. Mexican recycler shipping is ~45 days best case. (Background Val gave: he personally originated the bill-recycler concept for this industry and built one of the first at his prior company "Tashko"/TSC, where Yavorski and "Aleksey" learned recyclers — history, not current state.) Much of the recycler code can be implemented against the known spec with Claude in ~2–3 days. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Flex Pay ICOM module → refactor to ICOM-in-cloud, aligning with Flex Pay Trans.** Flex Pay's existing software (on the Vivan Sousen motherboard platform) keeps its ICOM-communication module on the kiosk itself, whereas Flex POS runs ICOM in the cloud (AWS). Pavel wants Dima or Sergey, when they free up, to refactor the Flex Pay / Flex Pay Dispenser ICOM module to the same ICOM-in-cloud design — big shared-code reuse and it converges the Flex Pay family on the Flex Pay Trans architecture. This ties into the unsettled test-stack/repo/deployment questions (the ~$80K/yr stack delta on the OMNI 2 section). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_pos_alpha_and_omni_2_architecture-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_POS_Alpha_and_OMNI_2_Architecture-April-15-2026.md]]

## Apr 15 2026 — FlexPay UX review with the new Product Manager; alpha/beta scope; Georgia go-to-market

Two segments of one continuous meeting ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-15-flex-pay-functionality-review.md|Flex-Pay Functionality Review]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]; recording stopped after an 8-minute orientation and restarted for a ~56-minute working review) walking Andrei's first-hand FlexPay kiosk observations (as a novice user) into product decisions. Andrei is now the transactional-products **Product Manager** (a corporate, not sales, role — confirmed [[wiki/meetings/2026-03-30-black-horse-invoice-payment-review.md|Mar-30]]). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-1-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-2-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-April-15-2026.md]]

- **Process pipeline + tooling (Val's standard).** Observations must climb: comments → capabilities & requirements → ADRs → specifications → prose → code. The default conversion path is **capture the transcript → Aaron's MCP for Jira + Aaron's MCP for Confluence → Jira capabilities/requirements** (with descriptions and acceptance criteria). Val cautioned against persona-driven user stories for a small team (documentation load) — be selective. Val also acknowledged JVL's docs/tooling are far from perfect (tribal-knowledge problem). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-1-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-April-15-2026.md]]
- **FlexPay UX decisions.** [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-2-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-April-15-2026.md]]
  - **Allow immediate ticket scan on the idle screen; move EN/ES language selection to the next screen** (today a "Start Play" tap is required first because language is chosen there). Either presenting a barcode OR tapping the screen should initiate validation — a capability change of "a matter of days"; keep educational messaging/a scanner sticker since scanning-first isn't instinctive to casino players. **Agreed.**
  - **Central barcode stays as-is — hardware constraint, no change.** Only the thinner side barcodes read at the reader; the reader's beam isn't wide enough and the landing space won't fit the large central barcode. Accepted as a learnable quirk.
  - **"$0 playable" after cash insert is incorrect — bug vs scope gap unclear.** Scanning a freshly-printed ticket at the kiosk showed $0 playable while scanning at the machine correctly showed $40. [[wiki/people/sebastian.md|Sebastian]] to pull kiosk + machine logs to classify; Val assigned product ownership to Andrei on the spot.
  - **PIN-creation screen (ships in alpha):** move the "Create PIN Code"/"Confirm PIN Code" labels directly above the PIN input box, and change the action-button text between states ("Enter" → "Confirm") so the create→confirm transition is noticeable. Deeper redesign (shrinking/relocating the instruction panel) deferred to beta.
  - **Ticket-validation PIN screen:** remove the Confirm button — auto-validate on the fourth digit, error on mismatch, advance on success (the universal phone pattern).
  - **Email-entry keyboard → QWERTY (from alphabetical) + a ".com" shortcut** — backlog, not alpha; the kiosk also supports receipt by SMS.
  - **Card-preview graphic → all-zero number + far-future expiry** — asset comes from [[wiki/organizations/incom.md|InComm]] (who makes the cards), so JVL asks InComm to replace it.
  - **Idle-screen buttons ("Scan Ticket"/"Add Funds") → gentle looped animations** pointing at the physical scanner and bill acceptor (both already work in idle without a button press). Parked pending Andrei posting a photo.
  - **Player-A/Player-B persistent-hands scenario is NOT a fix item.** A player abandoning a machine with accumulated hands, letting the next player inherit and cash out, is industry-standard behavior — players ("fleas") hunt such machines and the market accepts it. Val's framing: the ticket is a **temporary wallet** ("it's the same as somebody stealing your wallet"); a non-secure cash environment.
- **Alpha-vs-beta scope principle (Val).** Small polish ships in alpha; larger redesigns wait for beta so full market feedback is gathered and implemented in one cycle — engineering must not be occupied with aesthetics pre-alpha, or the product falls into a never-ending polish loop. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-2-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Georgia go-to-market timing.** Alpha release is blocked on **temporary plastic casings arriving in Georgia (~2 weeks out)**, so realistic alpha start is **~May 1**, window ~May 1–15; ~2 weeks of setup labor. Customer meetings begin ~**May 15** at the Georgia office with a demo configuration placing **FlexPort across multiple machines alongside an e-wallet config and some TITO-only machines** for direct comparison — the first showing of **Flex-POS** to customers (most have seen FlexPay/FlexPort at the gaming shows). Andrei should be on the ground doing hands-on install ("nothing hits home like the actual installation"). [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]] starts May 1 lining up the right customers for a mid-May "wine and cheese" lunch demo; leave-behind sales materials don't exist yet and must be produced. Best competitive intel comes from the **Georgia gaming show in August** (~500 attendees, local family show; go day two, learn — don't sell). Georgia chose not to regulate the product category, so competition is intense and scattered. Cross-links [[wiki/projects/georgia-market-rollout.md]] (Apr-15). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-2-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Pricing model — not yet on paper.** Val has ideas but nothing written or tested; a dedicated pricing-model call was scheduled for **Tuesday, April 21, 3:00 PM Eastern** (Val, Juan, Felipe, Andrei). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-2-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-April-15-2026.md]]
- **Role division (corporate umbrella vs sales).** Andrei is a **corporate entity** providing a marketing/corporate/market-intelligence/product-depth umbrella; [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]] runs day-to-day Georgia sales and fields price/timing/availability questions while Andrei absorbs and steps in when a customer talks multi-location, multi-market or corporate-scale (package deals across purchase/leasing/payment terms, other geographies). Crucially, Felipe cannot cut deals; Andrei can ("the mightiest stick"). In Texas, "Rich" runs sales similarly (a dedicated salesperson to be hired later) with Andrei supporting; in Nebraska, "Denis" does not pitch transactional products but can loop Andrei into a meeting. Val's coaching doctrine for meetings: "less is more" — absorb, take notes, follow up by phone (covert recording devices ruled out as illegal). **§15.8:** compensation percentages were mentioned (Felipe and Andrei each keep their own, non-overlapping) but are **NOT ingested** — role/market/product economics only, no named-person compensation figures. ("Rich" the Texas salesperson and the Nebraska "Denis" are named only in passing and left un-matched, no dangling links — noted in the batch-145 overview journal, not force-matched.) [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-2-April-15-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-April-15-2026.md]]
- **InComm reschedule.** Juan wants to reschedule an [[wiki/organizations/incom.md|InComm]] call so Andrei meets John and InComm updates JVL on its pipeline tech (ATM rollover, digital wallet) — no date committed. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex-Pay_functionality_review-1-April-15-2026.md]]

## Apr 17 2026 (call) — two pay stations for end of April, and Val's bet on the recycler

On a call with [[wiki/people/rich-winley.md|Rich Winley]] ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-17-rich-winley-qr-data-discipline-and-sales-hold.md]], 10:31 AM ET), three days before the Apr-20 debate below, Val gave his target and his lean. He says "pay station", not "FlexPay". *(Inference: it is the FlexPay kiosk of the earlier Rich calls.)* All from the transcript [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Rich_Winley_QR_Data_Discipline_and_Sales_Hold-April-17-2026.md]]:

- **Target.** "ninety percent probability that I will be done by the end of April with uh. The pay station. And I will probably have two pay stations, one with the dispenser and one with the recycler."
- **Not a size split.** Rich asked "For smaller locations?" Val: "recycler probably for bigger locations or or the recycler is probably for any location. And I believe that you guys will all of you will probably move to… a recycler rather than… dispenser."
- **The difference, as Val put it.** "dispenser you have to preload and reload… Recycler you don't… it always functions with… the stack of money that… it recirculates." Asked what happens when the money runs out: "The money in the recycler never runs out. It's being fed by the money that goes inside of the location." The player puts cash into the recycler, gets one e-wallet, walks "from one machine to the next with the same e wallet", and "when they want to cash in, the recycler gives them the money."
- **The dispenser stays available.** "if you guys wanna continue working with dispenser, it's fine with me."

## Apr 20 2026 — Genadi schedule review: HFM backlog + the dispenser-vs-recycler strategy debate

A ~1h 7m status/alignment call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-20-genadi-schedule-review-alignment.md|Genadi Schedule Review & Alignment]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]]) running the active Jira hardware backlog, then turning into a strategic product-direction discussion. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]

Val to confirm it's actually ordered and move the ticket from "To Do" to "In Progress." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]

[[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]

[[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]

Val recalled agreeing but was unsure of the resolution (he'd told Mikhail "give me one day, you work with him" and may have dropped the follow-up); he'll check status. The Minsk dispenser was received (~Friday) and Pavel + Biratka are integrating it (configuring for "candles"/свечи payout mode); the Georgia dispenser was to ship Friday with a larger Georgia shipment [[wiki/people/arkady-batson.md|Arkady]] would add it to free of charge. Val leans toward **air-shipping the Georgia dispenser** given Minsk's alpha is already live. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]
- **Strategic — FlexPay product definition + pricing paths.** Genadi described FlexPay as a side-by-side cabinet (narrower base, wider top, similar to the express-cabinet form) housing a printer card, cash dispenser, bill acceptor and scanner. Val laid out two go-to-market paths: (1) **support JVL's own FlexSport** directly with the product; or (2) **sell to distributors at a discount** (~$8,000) so they resell under their own brand at ~$11,000, with the distributor handling its own Pyramid-dongle arrangement. Rough economics (product/component only, no compensation): Val's COGS estimate ~$4,000 (Fujitsu printer ~$1,000–$1,080 + bill acceptor + a Vivo unit ~$2,000 + ~$1,500 in other components; Val computed $2,500 + $1,500). Genadi pushed back that distributor volumes are small (10–20–50 units) and questioned whether this helps JVL's core line or just pushes JVL into other OEMs' cabinets. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]
- **Strategic pivot — recycler + FlexSport over the cash dispenser.** Val proposed bundling: sell FlexPay (with recycler) at ~$12,000 and give away several **FlexSport** units per sale (FlexSport nominal retail ~$1,200, wholesale ~$990; JVL cost only ~$80–$100, so giving 10 away is cheaper than putting a printer — ~$300 Pyramid, ~$500 "Operator Nami" — in each machine); Val mused pricing FlexSport at $300 to match Pyramid. The stronger product is the **slim recycler-based next-gen (IP 1055)** — slim form factor, recycler printer, no operator, no moving parts (no paper, no print head to clean); Val believes a BOM ~$2,000–$3,000 sells at ~$6,000–$9,000, plus a **$99/month subscription and a mobile app** for operators to monitor fleet performance. Val leaned toward **abandoning the cash dispenser entirely**; Genadi shared the concern that the dispenser targets an existing market where JVL offers no clear benefit ("lots of effort for selling 3 units and more headaches") — but the call was **not locked**, parked for continued discussion all week. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]
- **Pyramid integration / dongle.** Existing gaming machines connect their Pyramid printers via **RS-232** with a proprietary Pyramid protocol; FlexSport sits on the **bill-validator line**, intercepting signals. To put a JVL dispenser into a machine that already has a Pyramid printer + bill acceptor, JVL's own printer and bill acceptor aren't needed — but the recycler or FlexPay must contain a **Pyramid dongle**, which requires a **contract with Pyramid**. Alternative: sell to Georgia wholesalers/distributors (Val cited 3–4 local manufacturers who'd want it but lack the technology) who handle their own integration. No decision. **Genadi's undertaking:** produce two schedules for **IP 1055** — a short ~2-week view and a longer-term roadmap — ideally that day or the next; Val confirmed IP 1055 exists with 3 features and that per schedule Genadi should start design. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_genadi_schedule_review_alignment-April-20-2026.md]]

## Apr 28 2026 — Flex-Recycler requirements/scope/acceptance: the vendor Java library that removed Alekseev, and the new-repo/GVL-API plan

The first of the Apr-28→30 Flex-Recycler REQ working sessions ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-28-recycler-architecture-planning.md|Recycler Architecture Planning]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]], [[wiki/people/alexander-biratka.md|Alexander Biratko]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina Shishko]]) — how to architect, scope, staff, and write acceptance criteria for the bill-recycler product on the Rockchip **RK3568** platform. Continues the recycler direction of the [[#apr-20-2026--genadi-schedule-review-hfm-backlog--the-dispenser-vs-recycler-strategy-debate|Apr 20]]/[[#apr-21-2026--genadi-continue-planning-sequencing-the-flex-slim-ip-1055-jira-gantt|Apr 21]] Genadi planning. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]

- **The discovery that flipped the staffing decision.** Val opened intending to give the recycler-protocol integration to [[wiki/people/evgeniy-alekseev.md|Evgeniy Alekseev]] (who had implemented, but never tested, the same MEI protocol for FlexPay). Mid-meeting, Genadi observed the recycler vendor is the same firm behind JVL's MEI bill validator and almost certainly ships a ready-made Java library; the team navigated to the vendor's GitHub live and confirmed a **2025-vintage Java library** (API + demo + console demo + integration toolkit) whose classes look near-identical to JVL's existing MEI integration. Their own MEI library is a 2016 build they decompiled/repacked over the years to fix bugs. Because no byte-level protocol work is needed — only a higher-level integration on top of the vendor library — **Alekseev is no longer needed for this work** (Val was explicit it's nothing against him). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **Architecture mirrors the existing kiosk stack.** Pavel: Linux on RK3568 → a Java backend that drives all workflow/operator/player logic → a browser-based GUI over a known IP. Alexander framed the change as "augment, not rewrite" — keep the architecture and existing screens, change only the recycler-specific operation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **New Flex_Recycler repo, assembled — not forked.** Val pushed back against forking FlexPOS because forks drag in dead code that pollutes and inflates Claude Code's context; Alexander agreed (dead Java code doesn't compile if unloaded but does clutter Claude). The plan: assemble the new product from selected parts of FlexPay/FlexDispenser, then use Claude Code to clean unneeded services/calls; the Linux base (RK3568 root) is consumed as a build artifact from its existing repo, not copied; a third repo may hold IO-board libraries. Pavel clarified the workflow being mirrored is **FlexDispenser's**, not FlexPOS's (customer/player flow almost one-for-one, only the bill acceptor is replaced). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **Reuse the gaming-machine IO board with FRAM depopulated.** Val objected to the board's cost; Genadi explained the price is almost all in the 1.5MB of FRAM needed for transactions. For the recycler, no-populating six of the FRAM chips (leaving one, plus a cheap 16-kbit part) drops the cost by ~$30 and yields an already-designed, already-tested board. Adds LED-controller functionality for door/switch monitoring. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **Firmware-language change deferred to FP7.** Val wants JVL to move "categorically away from C++" toward Rust; Pavel is skeptical about Rust for STM32 firmware specifically, though open to rewriting the OS-level library layer in Rust; Alexander defended Rust's cross-compilation. Genadi resolved the timing: the near-future **FP7** product needs the IO-board processor swapped to a high-speed-USB part (current one is becoming obsolete), so that is the natural moment to reconsider the firmware language and bring in a younger AI-routing engineer. Val explicitly did not want Alekseev on it. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **The GVL API — a Java interface with a not-implemented stub.** Alexander described the pattern: a Java interface file (methods like `dispenseBill`, `acceptBill`, `getStatus`, `loadCassette`) lives in the Flex_Recycler repo with a stub implementation returning "not implemented"; tests can be written against the interface before any implementation exists, and the work is done when every method is implemented. JVL provides the interface; the implementation (over the vendor library) is written in-house with Claude Code. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **Hardware logistics — two samples, one to Minsk.** Val confirmed **two** recycler samples were paid for (Genadi thought one); one goes to Georgia, the second should ship to Minsk so Alexander can integrate against the physical device (many bugs only surface with real hardware, per the dispenser experience). Genadi estimates ~5 weeks China→Belarus; Chinese import taxes paid via intermediaries are non-recoverable. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **CI/CD and acceptance tests from day one; unit tests demoted.** The infrastructure must include CI/CD alongside acceptance-criteria definition, because branches will break main once they start landing. Val stated unit tests are now largely obsolete in the Claude-Code era; Alexander affirmed integration and acceptance tests are the primary quality gate. Requirements-first: feed Claude requirements, code generation follows. [[wiki/people/andrey-bosik.md|Andrei Bosik]] to build a base-metrics "checker" during the buildout. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]
- **Requirements plan around Pavel's vacation.** Pavel's last working day is Thu Apr 30; Pavel + Alexander build a Jira sketch (~2 weeks of described work for Alexander) on Apr 29, with a 10:00 AM Apr 29 walk-through (plus Pavel's Figma user-flow link). On Mon May 4 (Pavel away), Val meets Alexander, [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]] (Product Owner, to expand sparse requirements), and [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]] (ramping as production requirements lead under Juan) to drill into requirements. UI: the new platform is slim with a **vertical screen**; [[wiki/people/ihar-hrybko.md|Igor Gribko]] will redo screens for the vertical layout, but not until requirements are locked. §15.8 clear (machine/component/logistics economics only; no named-person compensation). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_recycler_architecture_planning-April-28-2026.md]]

## Apr 29 2026 — Flex-Recycler development planning: IP-1059 stood up as its own product, and the REQ gaps that still need authoring

A notes-only engineering-planning session ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-29-flex-recycler-development-planning.md|Flex-Recycler Development Planning]]; attendees not listed) that **established Flex-Recycler as a separate product, IP-1059**, split out from the IP-1014 Flex-Cash Dispenser (separate repository, infrastructure, and software stack; owner [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_development_planning-April-29-2026.md]]

- **Product concept, as recorded:** "the same as Flex-Pay, but the Bill Validator + Card Dispenser pair is replaced by a single Cash Recycler, and the card payout is replaced by direct cash payout." Card-based redemption (payout onto a credit card) is excluded from MVP, to be confirmed with Val. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_development_planning-April-29-2026.md]]
- **The requirements gap that framed the session.** The existing REQs (REQ-1019..1027, REQ-1037) cover form factor, display, GPS, GLI, and audit print, but **do not describe the recycler's core business behaviour** — cassettes, denominations, and the service/replenishment workflow. The team flagged ~11 new REQs still to author: recycler business logic (minimum two cassettes, two denominations per cassette; only configured denominations accepted; payout from cassettes; error/state tracking), accepted-denomination configuration + player disclosure, the operator replenishment/recount workflow, GPS purpose/data model, telemetry over LTE (priority errors → low-cash → full-cassette, to a new microservices stack, not the legacy CMS), diagnostics/log retrieval (network primary + USB fallback), LED and sound effects, monitor resolution/aspect ratio, and a universal repository-infrastructure standard. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_development_planning-April-29-2026.md]]
- **Live Jira build-out under IP-1059.** The team drafted the Feature/Story list in Jira during the call — FP-527 repurposed ("Player GUI and Cash Recycler Workflow"), FP-530 ("Cash Recycler Workflow & GUI API"), and FP-534 (Cash Redemption/Acceptance, with FP-535/536/537) moved under IP-1059; FP-528 split into player-workflow vs operator/service-menu screens; a designer story for [[wiki/people/ihar-hrybko.md|Igor Gribko]]. Repository-infrastructure/CI-CD is owned by [[wiki/people/alexander-biratka.md|Alexander Biratko]]; HAL-library (door/tamper/power-state) integration by [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]]. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_development_planning-April-29-2026.md]]
- **Roadmap as discussed:** repository infrastructure starts the week of May 4; HAL integration ~2 days after; cash-recycler dispense work from the week of May 11; acceptance work + basic error handling late May; finalize positive/negative scenarios on the **real device in early June** (after delivery). Open items to raise with Val (audit-print MVP scope; card payout in/out; GPS purpose) and with [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]] (a modem with GPS — feasibility and available modules; bumping platform RAM 2 GB → 4 GB; native monitor resolution/aspect ratio; GPS running independently of the modem). §15.8: product/component/logistics economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_development_planning-April-29-2026.md]]

## Apr 29 2026 — Flex-Recycler pre-May4 REQ daily sync: four personas, and the e-wallet-not-TITO ruling

The evening "pre-May4 REQ" daily sync ([[wiki/meetings/2026-04-29-flex-recycler-req-daily-sync.md|Flex-Recycler pre-May4 REQ daily sync]]; [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val Levitan]]) — a requirements sprint toward a **May 4** milestone — reviewed Andrei's draft Personas document live and produced Val's long tutorial on why the Recycler architecture exists. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]

- **Personas cut five → four.** Val told Andrei twice he was "drowning" on unconventional personas (compliance/regulator, bad actor, insurance/risk officer, ADA) and to "scrap them all." The four locked personas: **Player; Location Owner / Site Attendant (merged, owner listed first); Technician; Route Operator.** Player is priority #1. Each persona is structured as brief description / what they care about / how they interact with the Recycler / red flags they cannot tolerate. Val flagged the Operator persona's "what they care about" section as missing and the Player section as "a little bit skimpy." [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]
- **e-wallet is the umbrella, not TITO.** Val and Juan walked Andrei off the TITO frame (a 30+-year IGT casino design needing a central server, kilometres of SAS wiring, redundant server rooms, and full-time staff; entry ~$900K for a 500-machine casino, most >$1.5M — "definitely not a gas station"). The credit-transfer abstraction is an **e-wallet** covering any concrete token — printed QR, barcode, four-digit PIN, plastic card, phone-photographed QR, or SMS-delivered PIN: "there's no standard, no rule." Auth = **QR paired with a PIN**. The token is **seized/confiscated on machine entry** and re-issued on cash-out (not transferable across machines without re-issue). An SMS-delivered PIN would drop the printer requirement entirely. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]
- **Why the Recycler exists (Val's deep-dive).** A win means the player walks away with cash, which needs a float; conventionally every machine has a bill acceptor + cash box, so cash-out needs a trusted transfer (a ticket) → hence TITO + server + drop teams sorting bills by denomination + armored refill + locked-up float. The Flex-Recycler eliminates almost all of it — remove bill acceptors/printers from floor machines (saving conservatively ~$1,000/machine), store incoming bills in cassettes that auto-refill the dispensing cassette; because RTP favours the house the Recycler stays filled; two recyclers can sit in one body for busy sites. Bills need "ATM-grade cash" (slightly worn, sorted; brand-new bills vacuum-stick). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]
- **Deployment rules.** **No co-installation with machine-side bill acceptors** (they would drain the Recycler) and **no co-installation with third-party redemption kiosks** (Count-R, distributed by Vanilla) — a Flex-Recycler location must be a **JVL-only ecosystem**, with player re-education/signage directing players to the Recycler first. Mixed-manufacturer floors are supported via **meter-pulse sniffing + bill-acceptor tap-in**, and JVL's **Flexport** window is verified compatible with the four main Georgia cabinet suppliers. Phase-one go-to-market = **new/greenfield locations** (warehouse-prepped without bill acceptors/printers); retrofits are phase two. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]
- **Revenue model + market frame.** Recurring revenue = a **connectivity fee** to the operator (server/analytics/telemetry/low-cash alerts) + a **processing fee** on transactions to the player (as in Georgia); Juan wants JVL to consider a **lease-with-deposit** model (matching Vanilla/Pace). Income (debit card) confirmed it will enter both Texas and Nebraska with JVL (~$2.50/transaction + ~$5 ATM fee), but US players strongly prefer cash. Market sizing: Georgia (24th, ~40,000 machines) vs Texas (2nd-largest state), which Juan projects "could easily have 150,000 machines"; the **launch target is the AMOA show, March 2027.** [[raw/Meeting_Notes_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]
- **Texas compliance signal.** The only Texas compliance hook Juan could name: some manufacturers' kiosks do **driver's-license age verification** (under-21/no-ID forfeits the cash-out ticket), which JVL does not support; Juan to ask his Texas contact **Rich Winley** (currently testing JVL games machine-side) whether it is actually required, and whether transactional cash-out fees are charged in Texas today. §15.8: product/market/machine economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_flex_recycler_req_daily_sync-April-29-2026.md]]

## May 1 2026 — Flex-Slim device layout: the front-panel stack order and the reuse-existing-molds speed call

A notes-only working session ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-01-flex-slim-device-layout.md|Flex-Slim Device Layout]]; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]) finalising the physical front-panel layout of the **Flex-Slim paystation** — a ~11.5"-wide single-touchscreen slim variant of the paystation, with scanner, printer and bill validator/recycler stacked vertically. This continues the slim-recycler product line from the [[#mar-11-2026--the-flex-recycler-slim-a-narrow-between-machines-payout-kiosk|Mar-11 "Flex Recycler Slim"]] and the [[#apr-21-2026--genadi-continue-planning-sequencing-the-flex-slim-ip-1055-jira-gantt|Apr-21 IP 1055 sequencing]]. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Slim_Device_Layout-May-1-2026.md]]

- **Front-panel stack order — decided.** Top-to-bottom: **scanner at the top → printer in the middle → bill validator/recycler raised as high as possible** (to the upper-middle area where the hand naturally rests). This departs from the competitor convention (printer-on-top) precisely because the slim newly includes a scanner: Andrei argued from ergonomics that the two most-frequent/critical actions — inserting cash and scanning a QR code — must sit at a comfortable height for an average ~1.75–1.78 m adult without bending, while receiving a printed ticket is a tolerable third-priority action. The printer has the largest envelope and slides forward for paper reload, so it dictates much of the geometry. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Slim_Device_Layout-May-1-2026.md]]
- **Single dispenser only; width defines "slim."** Total width is ~11.5" (<12"). Two cash dispensers won't fit (would need ~18", negating "slim"), so **two-dispenser configurations stay on the full-size paystation** and the Flex-Slim is single-dispenser only. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Slim_Device_Layout-May-1-2026.md]]
- **Reuse the existing paystation screen-angle molds — speed over profile.** Val dislikes the current side profile (rear hump from printer depth) but explicitly endorsed shipping it: reusing the existing molds saves **~2 months to market**, the profile is mostly invisible when units are sandwiched between machines, and "we can always make another model." Visible head-on differentiation stays: an edge-to-edge full-width touchscreen and programmable **RGB GoFlash perimeter light bands**. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Slim_Device_Layout-May-1-2026.md]]
- **Field-replaceable bezel glass.** The single-piece molded top holds Gorilla glass on a recessed plastic shelf via double-sided tape, decoupled from the LCD (no optical bonding) — so a scratched cover is a cheap drop-in sub-assembly swap (a margin-bearing replacement SKU) without disturbing the screen. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Slim_Device_Layout-May-1-2026.md]]
- **Open items (Genadi).** Add a **liquid/spill drainage channel** where the recessed glass meets the top housing (runoff destination TBD — Val flagged inevitable drink spills), and **verify both GSM (flat feed + top stacker) and MEI (downward feed) recyclers fit** the new vertical layout with enough clearance for either profile. §15.8: device/component/market economics only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Slim_Device_Layout-May-1-2026.md]]

Andrei catches up on the capability/requirement/use-case side while Alexander (Minsk reopening May 5) begins developing features in parallel. A Russian-language [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] ↔ [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]] call ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-05-flex-pos-recycler-web-interface-planning.md|Flex-POS / Recycler Web Interface Planning]]) opened the next workstream: a single JVL **operator web interface** that surfaces the Recycler, POS, Flex-Pay and any other JVL ecosystem hardware in one UI, with the **operator persona** (an operator running a Recycler + POS) as the primary lens. The Georgia-trip half of the call is on [[wiki/projects/georgia-market-rollout.md]] (May-5). ## May 18 2026 — GA trip recap: competitor kiosks, the FlexPay gaps, the merchant-number question, and the POS name

The product half of the [[wiki/meetings/2026-05-18-ga-trip-recap-kiosk-intel.md|May-18 GA trip recap]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]]). The market half is on [[wiki/projects/georgia-market-rollout.md]] (May-18). Only the recap segment of the recording (from 01:27:13) is used. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_Trip_Recap_Kiosk_Intel-May-18-2026.md]]

- **What competitor kiosks look like (Andrei, field view).** About "five major manufacturers" cover most of the market. They aim for a **tower** design with the touch screen at about shoulder height. Most support TITO, receipt printing, and a small card terminal to "swipe your regular debit or credit card" and move money onto a player e-wallet, ticket or player card. Most also support **gift-card refilling**, and some offer a **split** (part of the win to a gift card, the rest stays as TITO). The metal "feels like it's a real ATM" — "much more protected than our current version of" FlexPay. Andrei will share photos. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_Trip_Recap_Kiosk_Intel-May-18-2026.md]]
- **Val's focus ruling.** When Andrei asked whether a standalone kiosk is needed at all, Val moved the talk back to the committed products (FlexPay kiosk, POS, FlexPort, Recycler). See [[wiki/decisions/2026-05-18-stay-on-the-committed-transactional-pipeline.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_Trip_Recap_Kiosk_Intel-May-18-2026.md]]
- **Andrei's three FlexPay risks — and Flex Slim.** FlexPay cannot refill gift cards, accepts only cash (no debit/credit), and is "too massive" next to the slim towers. Val accepted the form-factor point: "flex slim as we call it recycler… doesn't have to have the actual recycler. It could have just a regular flex pay functions in it… So flex slim addresses that." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_Trip_Recap_Kiosk_Intel-May-18-2026.md]]
- **Card acceptance — "have you actually observed this?"** Andrei saw kiosks "in location and in… warehouse of one of our operators, the biggest one" fitted with debit/credit readers, but "did not use my credit card for that purpose". Juan: taking a card and converting it to cash is "the holy grail that every casino wants" and "almost the opposite of everything [Georgia] is trying to protect"; Val: it "would go against federal legislation". Felipe said he had been told a **debit** card can buy a TITO ticket (not a credit card) and will test it. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_Trip_Recap_Kiosk_Intel-May-18-2026.md]]
- **The merchant-number doctrine (Val's understanding, stated with "to the best of my knowledge").** Placing winnings onto the prepaid Mastercard is InComm's side; taking money **out** of it needs a Mastercard **merchant number**, and "each kiosk that you put is a merchant". Val believes "nobody has a merchant number with Mastercard" in this business, because a merchant needs a business category and "Gambling… federally, is prohibited". His reason Mastercard stays out: fear of a federal class action ("a husband gambles his wages away and the wife places a complaint"); "The biggest casinos in the business… cannot get that." "But if it is available, we'll just follow the pattern." Juan: the word "gaming" is what triggers the rejection on a card transaction. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_Trip_Recap_Kiosk_Intel-May-18-2026.md]]
- **Buying a product vs buying credits.** Felipe described using a gift card at a "Mims Amusement" kiosk to buy credit onto a shared player's card (a $2 transaction charge). Val's reading: then "you actually are buying a physical substance… it's not gambling business. Then you buying a product" — and whether that works "depends on the merchant number". [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_Trip_Recap_Kiosk_Intel-May-18-2026.md]]
- **Gift-card refill is a service capability, not a kiosk feature.** Val: "It's not a kiosk feature… it's a capability of a service provider… either Incomm or Mastercard. And it becomes available to anyone through an API." If it is turned on, "we will automatically fit into it". Felipe: cards cannot be reloadable until **July 1**; Val: "They have an aim date of July. It keeps on sliding." Juan: reading and writing the card is "a function of our protocol with income"; turning refill on would be "a week's worth of work for the engineering team", provided there is a card swiper on the device — Val: "there is a swiper on it definitely." Val also noted the observed readers were magnetic swipe plus barcode, "not a chip". [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_Trip_Recap_Kiosk_Intel-May-18-2026.md]]
- **Prepaid card → prize card → ATM, and where the Recycler fits.** Val: even taking a prepaid Mastercard to an ATM "is not allowed" to his knowledge, "that's why they convert it to a prize card. So then you can take prize card to an ATM. And this is where Recycler comes in." Juan recalled InComm saying the existing gift card converts to an additional card for an extra fee of "two fifty". [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_Trip_Recap_Kiosk_Intel-May-18-2026.md]]
- **Pax and reconciliation — Val: "Pax is not a competitor if we do our job correctly."** Val drew the line: the Pax is a handheld where the win "must be entered manually" (Andrei: "That's the weakest part of it"). Issue two: coupling the device with **operator intelligence** — "a big thing that nobody is addressing today". On reconciliation, Val walked through a mismatch (MudaGoose says nine thousand, Pax says ten) and asked how it is resolved; Juan: camera review and hours of work, or "You let it go until the number gets really big". Val's small-loss arithmetic: "Dollar per ticket… Ten tickets, ten dollars. Three hundred sixty five days, three thousand six hundred and fifty dollars" (per location). *(The derived notes' "$18K/year/location" is not in the transcript* [[raw/Meeting_Notes_GA_Trip_Recap_Kiosk_Intel-May-18-2026.md]]*.)* Juan: an operator would prefer one solution for all reporting — Val: "that's what our POS is capable of doing"; Andrei: FlexPay too. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_Trip_Recap_Kiosk_Intel-May-18-2026.md]]
- **The POS name.** Operators hear "POS" as a store till; Val ruled to "depart from naming it P, O S". See [[wiki/decisions/2026-05-18-rename-flex-pos-away-from-point-of-sale.md]]; it became Flex-IQ by May 25 (below). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_Trip_Recap_Kiosk_Intel-May-18-2026.md]]
- **Gift-card refill on the roadmap, not in the demo.** Andrei: FlexPay "covers everything that market needs now" apart from form factor, with refill "to be defined". Juan: "we're definitely going to support it"; Andrei: "but we cannot demonstrate it right now". Val: nobody can demonstrate live refill yet — "we can fake it"; "if that feature is available, of course we're going to do it." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_GA_Trip_Recap_Kiosk_Intel-May-18-2026.md]]
- *(Later evolution, not a contradiction: on the Jul-22 process-flow review the wiki records PAX named "the true competitor" — see that section below.)*

§15.8: product/device/market economics only; no named-person pay.

## May 22 2026 — transactional MVP strategy: three-pronged market entry, pricing vs Pax, cash-is-king, and the sales doctrine

A wide-ranging, notes-only strategic alignment ([[wiki/meetings/2026-05-22-transactional-mvp-strategy.md|Transactional MVP Strategy]], ~1h 28m, 4:04pm ET; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan Martinez]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]). It began as Andrei's request for a defined one-week priority focus before his Texas trip; Val pushed back that isolated, uninterrupted focus is unachievable in a small North-American company where departments can't be walled off — then laid out the transactional market-entry framework in full. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]

- **Three-pronged MVP path (Val, locked).** The product web spins out from three products — **Flex IQ + Operator's Dashboard + Tito Cloud Server**. Juan wanted the **Flex Card Dispenser** in phase one (the POS must output the card); Val agreed but placed the dispenser **shortly after** phase one, not in it. Together, IQ + dispenser + dashboard sharply reduce fraud and simplify operations; even operators who say "I have no fraud" or "I know everything myself" are, in Val's view, wrong — an "Apple-ish" minimal, flawless IQ + dashboard is a powerful entry point, and the discipline is "taking our time now and doing it right." [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **How Flex IQ works.** IQ sits **between the slot machine and the printer**, intercepting the Moo the Goose ticket cash-out signal. The attendant validates and activates the gift card on IQ; if the customer wants a receipt, the attendant presses a button and IQ **commands the existing Moo the Goose thermal printer** to print on its existing roll — **no new printer needed** (today Moo the Goose prints only the ticket). IQ has **two screens** (one customer-facing, one attendant-facing) plus a hidden internal connector for a **third remote screen**, used in Georgia locations where protection glass separates attendant from customer and the customer-facing screen must be mirrored outside the glass. Gift-card detail does **not** live in IQ itself — IQ issues the card and pushes everything to the **Payment Hub** (JVL's in-house service that interfaces to Microsoft Business Central); the Payment Hub is a hub, **not** an accounting station (it issues no invoices and reconciles nothing). The attendant can also **edit the cash-out value** IQ receives (a design feature and a security advantage, supporting trusted-player negotiations). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Operator-dashboard philosophy — ship a "skeleton" first.** Val wants the dashboard to launch showing the **full future layout with fields visible even when empty**, then populate real features weekly; operators are starved for information and even a correctly-structured wireframe will "make them salivate." Val deferred to Andrei as the dashboard expert (Andrei's HTML draft validated as the right direction). A minimal IQ-only dashboard (no SMIB, no Flexport) can already show everything Moo the Goose outputs (the existing accounting/data source), plus IQ service-state health and gift-card issuance detail. Juan's own view: most dashboards he's seen "sucked," so his method is to export data into SQL and build the reports he actually wants — not what the vendor wants shown. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Pricing vs Pax (program-level economics).** The standard operator objection: Pax costs ~$300, JVL IQ will sell for ~$1,000 — why pay 3x? Val's answer: Pax enables theft (a cash transaction on a register is "another way of stealing money"); IQ + dashboard delivers theft prevention and **account reconciliation** (which Pax lacks), plus the upgrade path to **SMIB** (~$10/machine) and **Flexport** (~$1,000 retail) that eventually replaces Moo the Goose entirely. Upsell sequence: IQ → dashboard → SMIB ("with every IQ I'll give you 6 free SMIBs") → Flexport → loyalty cards/promotions/mail-out card validation. A marketing lever Val floated: **"JVL dollars"** — convert part of a port's cost into JVL store credit redeemable for spare parts, machines, IQ units, or more ports. §15.8: product/market pricing only; no named-person compensation. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **"Cash is king" thesis.** Val: "Fuck the automation … cash is king." The moment a card is issued, the player converts it to cash — either at an ATM (~$5 ATM fee on top of a ~$2 card fee) or via the attendant, who can buy the $50-face card back for **$48** (cheaper than the ATM for the player, keeps them at the machine, builds the relationship, and the attendant earns the spread). Tito and e-wallet matter precisely because they **prevent a cash-out event**; Moo the Goose **forces** one (you get a ticket — "the worst event in a casino's life"), which structurally creates the conditions for illegal cash-redemption. Buying a **debit card with value is legal**, which legalises the whole transaction chain. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Texas / Nebraska market context.** Nebraska uses the same **Intralot** system as Georgia, but its Department of Revenue gives manufacturers no operator reports, so operators there are starved for data; **Texas** has no regulator and no reporting obligation at all — operators know cash collected but not losses, machine overpayments, or performance. Both markets are "dying for information." Gift cards are sold everywhere (Walgreens, CVS, pharmacies), are usable in Texas, and are not blocked by Nebraska's regs; the pitch in these states is **cost savings** — switching cash to cards cuts refill labor, kiosk maintenance and cash exposure, and a new Texas location can launch **card-only** with no cash at all. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Operator-vs-location dynamic.** Operators and locations distrust each other: locations see operators as "idiots making money in spite of themselves," operators see locations as "extortionists" for owning gas stations. Operators want fast bank-account transfer and the location's role minimised; locations want to keep cash exposure and their personal player relationships. This plays especially well in Nebraska and Texas, where operators are trying to **own their own locations** (as in Pennsylvania). On automation, Andrei proposed a fully-automated IQ (customer scans the ticket, takes a card from a dispenser, swipes it himself); Juan noted that breaks the **trusted human relationship** Georgia operators specifically want (only two humans allow "sell me your $50 card for $45"). Val's ruling: it depends on the market (German spielhalle locations are fully automated) — JVL stays **adaptive** (automated / attended / hybrid) per operator preference, and the near-term priority is to **break as few existing cycles as possible**: Moo the Goose is in ~6,500 locations at ~75% market share for 20+ years, and operators won't switch unless forced. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Sales doctrine — "don't sell, listen."** Val's rule for operator visits: don't arrive with a pre-programmed pitch; open with "we have a transactional product family — what are your pain points?" and let the operator describe problems while the salesperson maps which JVL product fits. Coming in with "you need an IQ" can fail because it may demand an operational-model change the operator hasn't accepted. He approved Andrei's Texas expo flyer as a **family-of-products introduction** (no prices, no specific solutions), and was emphatic: "If I see you coming in with a PowerPoint presentation, I'm going to fire you." Be yourself — "either beef or pork, not both." Andrei (not Felipe) is the Texas booth contact for transactional products; the flyer uses AI-rendered machine images (built from blueprints plus a FlexPay reference photo). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **PrizePay and the skill-games/casino line.** Andrei asked about the PrizePay card video Felipe shared; Val confirmed PrizePay is **Incom's reloadable second card**, redeemable at an ATM for a service fee (~$5 in the video). Juan flagged that converting gift cards directly to cash sounds illegal — it crosses the line separating skill games from casinos, which **Mike Patel (GOMOA president)** is challenging because the industry doesn't want federal heat. Val explained the video's operator can do it because he **owns his own ATM** and uses a **non-Incom processor**, putting him outside the regulated chain — and the regulator publicly said it enforces nothing at the kiosk, only at the machine. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **US legal/cultural framework (Val's extended aside to Andrei).** The US is a **constitutional republic**, not a top-down federal-law system: federal and state law, many legislators, interest groups, and courts interpreting the constitution, so the regulatory "pendulum" swings widely over long stretches. "In the US, laws are recommendations" (Juan) — "as long as you pay your taxes" (Val). Two illustrations: Canadian gambling law was **unwritten until 1996** because the 1935 criminal code never defined screens or slot machines ("and we made a shitload of money"); and Seminole tribal gaming began in **1978** in tents and tiki huts with ~300 pull-tab machines — federally illegal but legal on tribal land (tribes are foreign governments inside the US) — growing to **$2.5B/year** by the time Juan left as VP of operations. The lesson for the skill-games loophole: it's clever, it generates state tax revenue (**Georgia takes $1 of every $5 service fee**), and operators will lobby, fund politicians and pay for new legislation if anyone threatens it. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
- **Next capability grooming + near-term sessions.** Val named **Tito Cloud Server** (capability #60, already ~5–6 requirements attached) as the next capability to groom, with **Remote Initial Setup** the "next big deal" after (currently "chaotic and spotty," needs major grooming for elegance). Juan committed to weekend **sample dashboard reports** in two phases (phase 1 = Moo the Goose + Incom card-transaction data only; phase 2 = with SMIB + port). Near-term calls set: Monday **May 25 8am** Minsk call on IQ-MVP completeness (Val + Pavel also invited), and Tuesday **May 26 10am** Payment Hub deep-dive (Andrei + Juan + Nathan) to work out which accounting data flows where. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Transactional_MVP_Strategy-May-22-2026.md]]
