- **How Fiery Pay is understood to work.** No printer; it ties into the machine's **bill acceptor** (via a protocol like **JCM or MEI**), because the bill acceptor is what issues credits. Felipe's observed flow at a location ~2 hours away (near Macon): cash into the bill acceptor → play → on cash-out the credits go onto a **loyalty/e-wallet card** sitting on the machine → the player goes to a **kiosk** that converts player points to a **gift card** (a **$2 fee**) or a gift card back to player points. The gift card is a standard prize-pay / MasterCard-type card spendable anywhere (e.g. Walmart), but the player can also bring it back, scan the barcode, convert to player points, and load credits onto a machine; partial amounts split between gift card and machine. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Fiery_Pay_Competitive_Analysis-August-12-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Fiery_Pay_Competitive_Analysis-August-12-2026.md]]
- **Why the team sees it as weak and risky.** Val called the send-credits-back-to-a-machine step a **"legal-ish" gymnastics workaround** — operators treat it as a "transfer" point rather than a redemption point. He and Juan flagged heavy **fraud exposure** (money can be sent back to an unattended machine someone else then sits at; no real systems check on amounts), and Val's larger point: after all the gymnastics the player still walks away with a **cash-spendable gift card**, defeating the purpose of keeping money in play. Juan noted the space is essentially **unregulated** — GLC has said it does not control what these kiosks do. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Fiery_Pay_Competitive_Analysis-August-12-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Fiery_Pay_Competitive_Analysis-August-12-2026.md]]
- **Open legal question.** Nobody could explain how a **non-cash MasterCard gift card is legally converted into player points/cash** — it appears to need a merchant ID / reseller status and looks like a "fake transaction" run to empty the card; unclear whether Fiery Pay or the location holds the merchant ID. **Fiery Payments** is a broader payments company (ATM + card processing across Visa, MasterCard, Amex, Discover, Union Pay, JCB), not just a COAM product. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Fiery_Pay_Competitive_Analysis-August-12-2026.md]]
- **Market read on competitor redemption systems.** Felipe hears appetite for this kind of system from several operators; the "Tito" hype is cooling. Solutions now talked about: **Batson (Light & Wonder)** — *(name-collision flag: this "Batson" is a Light & Wonder product, NOT [[wiki/people/arkady-batson.md|Arkady Batson]] the JVL person — do not conflate)*; **Easylinks** (ticket printed in the back); **Fiery Pay** (Afshad / Aristocrat link — the most popular); and **Access** (with printer). One location Felipe recently serviced is pulling out its printers and kiosk to install a competitor system; Felipe saw the box labelled "**JVL kit for flex**," which he found odd. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Fiery_Pay_Competitive_Analysis-August-12-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Fiery_Pay_Competitive_Analysis-August-12-2026.md]]
- **JVL's answer — recycler + flex port around the "replay" wallet.** JVL's **"replay"** feature is its version of the temporary wallet. To print the initial QR code the location needs a **recycler** (not necessarily a FlexPay kiosk). Val's stated winning combination is a **recycler + a flex port** (which also enables sophisticated operator reporting); **IQ is optional** ("you probably don't need it," though Val expects operators will still take it). The recycler solution depends on InComm's price-play card. Val relayed a message received during the call from **John Hasel at Income** (= [[wiki/organizations/incom.md|InComm]]'s Jason Hall, per the same-day [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-12-incomm-skill-pay-recycler-strategy.md|InComm call]]): the current card is **Prize Play / Prize Pay**; the new one is **Skill Pay**, available from **mid-September**, which would let JVL implement the full solution around **November**. Val never spoke directly with Afshad (Afshad refused to sign the NDA); the back-and-forth was on Felipe's side. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Fiery_Pay_Competitive_Analysis-August-12-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Fiery_Pay_Competitive_Analysis-August-12-2026.md]]

**Decision (direction, no formal sign-off):** JVL's competitive package for these locations is a **recycler + a flex port**, built around the "replay" temporary wallet and InComm's price-play/Skill Pay card; **IQ optional**, not required. **§15.8:** clear — competitor/market/device economics only; no named-person compensation.

## Aug 14 2026 — first full Flex-Recycler cash cycle works; the MEI recycler is scrapped

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-14-flex-recycler-cycle-test-mei-rework.md|Aug-14 12:00 PM ET alignment call]] (~9 min; [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]], [[wiki/people/irina-shishko.md|Irina]]) Andrei reported the first full recycler cash cycle, and Val decided to drop the current MEI recycler. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Recycler_Cycle_Test_MEI_Rework-August-14-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_Recycler_Cycle_Test_MEI_Rework-August-14-2026.md]]

- **The cycle worked end to end.** Yesterday the team completed a full assembly and connected the recycler to **two gaming machines fitted with the new flex ports**. Use case: a player put **$5** into the cash dispenser, created and confirmed a PIN, and printed a **TITO-style banknote ticket (not a QR code)**; at a flex-port machine they scanned the ticket, confirmed the PIN, transferred $5 (5 credits), played, won **$15**, and cashed the $15 back to the e-wallet; back at the recycler they cashed out real money. Because the bank held only one $5 note, the machine dispensed at most $5 and showed a $10 balance still playable. Val called it "excellent news." [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Recycler_Cycle_Test_MEI_Rework-August-14-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_Recycler_Cycle_Test_MEI_Rework-August-14-2026.md]]
- **What Val wants tested next.** The first run used only one machine; Val wants the test re-run across **both machines, traversing back and forth a couple of times**, to prove the e-wallet communicates as a player moves between machines, then cash out the full balance (kept under $50) showing mixed bills — an ideal **$55 payout of two $20s and three $5s** (this setup has no $10s). First Andrei must finish the assembly: boot the dispenser (the latest build from **Alexander** is uploaded — which Alexander unspecified; likely [[wiki/people/alexander-biratka.md|Alexander Biratko]] the recycler build lead, not owner-confirmed, flagged), confirm it sees all parameters/parts, then finalize the recycler while Minsk is online. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Recycler_Cycle_Test_MEI_Rework-August-14-2026.md]]
- **Decision — scrap the MEI recycler, build the right one.** Val ruled the **MEI recycler's mechanics and construction are wrong** and JVL will not use it; there is no time to get the correct unit before the show, but the proper recycler — one that **supports multiple cassettes** — will be built instead. MEI will not supply the product; JVL already has it. The unit is being **sent to Minsk to be redone**, targeting a **first beta sample around mid-September**, timed to coincide with **InComm's Skill Pay card** introduction (continuing the Aug-12 recycler answer above). [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_Recycler_Cycle_Test_MEI_Rework-August-14-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_Recycler_Cycle_Test_MEI_Rework-August-14-2026.md]]

## Aug 18 2026 — Fiery Pay from its hardware maker's side, the QR-first idea, and how few players walk out with a ticket

At a show-week [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-18-mutha-goose-maker-supplier-dinner.md|dinner]] [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] questioned the maker of the Mutha Goose ([[wiki/organizations/rks.md|RKS]] by context) about the card systems now in Georgia. Mostly social; these are the business points. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Mutha_Goose_Supplier_Dinner_Intel-August-18-2026.md]]

- **The maker built Fiery Pay's in-machine hardware.** Val: "what do you have for the card system?" — "The Fiery payment." / "Is it yours? Yeah. Yes." — "We did all the hardware except for that, the prebuilt POS." An outside contractor did the POS side; "I did everything from the USB port to the games." Fiery Payments "wanted to leverage our existing… structure" — the Mutha Goose layout. Val had thought "Fiery payment was uh an Indian thing" ("Yeah, it is"). This adds a hardware supplier to the Aug-12 picture above (Afshad/Aristocrat); it does not contradict it. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Mutha_Goose_Supplier_Dinner_Intel-August-18-2026.md]]
- **How the card is used.** "The Fiery Pay uses the card is not as mobile money, not a gift card per se. They collect on machine number four, then they go up there and put it on machine number two. They're just using the card as the equivalent of a QR code." There is no reader in the machine: "Fiery Payments does not, it's all on the car[d]." The **next step**, which he was not sure he could share: "There's a QR code on every card so they can load up their card, Get their card issued in a second." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Mutha_Goose_Supplier_Dinner_Intel-August-18-2026.md]]
- **Why cards and not QR codes — the lottery.** He had built a QR-code system "but nobody wanted it. Once… the lottery said gift cards, they all went crazy." That system was done with IFS ("the internal financial services" — see [[wiki/people/bob.md]]): a standalone kiosk, and the QR code set up "a virtual bank account" — a packet to the server saying "this UUID is attached to nine hundred bucks", usable on a game or for an in-store purchase. Val: "I'll go look at IFS tomorrow." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Mutha_Goose_Supplier_Dinner_Intel-August-18-2026.md]]
- **Val's QR-first flow.** Val asked why not issue a QR code first, play across machines, and only issue the gift card at the end. The maker: "That would make more sense, yeah… A QR code to start, and then when you're done, issuing the gift card. Makes more sense." Inna raised that this brings back a printer; answer: "One printer… one at the counter… or kiosks." A side remark about "what Farmers Bank is doing with their card being the mobile money" is **unclear**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Mutha_Goose_Supplier_Dinner_Intel-August-18-2026.md]]
- **Cash-out rates — how few tickets are printed.** Val's working numbers: a machine makes "on an average a hundred dollars per day net win", pays out "approximately ninety percent", so it takes in about $1,000 and pays out $900. [[wiki/organizations/incom.md|InComm]] (transcribed "income") told him cash-outs run "approximately one point five tickets per day". Inna: "one and a half tickets a day per machine sounds right?" — maker: "It about does." Most players "play it off… They don't leave with anything"; a ticket walks out "whatever somebody hits big" (his example: a $5,000 hit). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Mutha_Goose_Supplier_Dinner_Intel-August-18-2026.md]]
- **The $50 figure is not confirmed.** Val: "I think fifty bucks a month is what he charges per period for your location." Maker: "Fifty bucks per location?" Val then says "you're talking about a fiery plate" and the maker answers only for his own system ("no work charges"). Who "he" is, and whether $50/location/month is a Fiery Pay price, is **unclear**. The derived notes state it as a Fiery Pay price; the transcript does not support that. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Mutha_Goose_Supplier_Dinner_Intel-August-18-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Mutha_Goose_Supplier_Dinner_Intel-August-18-2026.md]]

**§15.8:** clear — product, market and fee talk only; no one's pay. Social and family talk at the dinner is not recorded.

## Aug 21 2026 — the GAMOA show read on Flex IQ: wrapped cards break the reader, and everyone asks for replay and split

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-21-post-goam-show-recap.md|Aug-21 post-GAMOA recap]] (recording 1, 4:01–5:45 PM ET; [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]], [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]]) Andrei reported back from the Georgia show. The ruling that came out of it is [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-21-release-flex-iq-as-is-place-units-collect-facts.md]].

- **Market reaction to the device itself was strong.** Operators "really like the design," reach to touch and tap it, and when they lift it "they feel that it is kind of heavy, well-built, and they admit it." They immediately understand the value of a transaction that needs no manual input — "it avoids fraud and mistakes." But asked directly whether they see fraud or mistyping with PAX today, "They do not admit it. They say, no, it works well. We don't see any issues." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
- **The wrapped-card blocker.** InComm's product manager for the cards told Andrei that cards shipped **for a kiosk** (locked in a protected box, unreachable by cashier or player) go out **unwrapped as a plain stack**, but cards kept at a cashier desk or otherwise publicly accessible may **only** ship **wrapped** — so the number is not visible. IQ's magnetic-stripe/swipe verification therefore cannot activate them: "right now IQ cannot work with unwrapped cards and it will never be allowed by income." Andrei's two options: add a **dispenser** to IQ, or make IQ **read barcodes** (the wrap carries a visible barcode). Juan: "I think it should support both, anyway." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
  - Physically, the existing optical reader sits underneath and cannot be presented with the wrap's barcode; Val walked Andrei through rotating and inserting the pack diagonally on camera, could not confirm a read (the unit was switched off), and concluded "It's okay, I know how to do it… we can install a second barcode reader. Not a big deal." A **USB 2D barcode scanner** (Juan) or a hand "gun scanner" (Felipe) is the interim fallback. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
  - Andrei found an upside in wrapped cards: per InComm, activating a wrapped card "equals automatically to the point that player has already accepted the… rules," so **no player-facing screen is needed** — a cashier-facing screen suffices. Juan still wanted the amount loaded shown to the player; Andrei: "it shouldn't be touch. It might be something simpler." Felipe noted PAX prints a receipt showing what was loaded (he had collected from a PAX once and got one). Andrei's conclusion: "if we will be able to print out tickets, we don't need a player-facing screen." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
- **The replay / split demand.** "every single person we talked to, every single operator master, when we were discussing IQs, they said, does it do replay?" — meaning moving a player from one machine to another. Separately "many of them are asking… can you split the transaction?" so a player gets part on the card and the rest as cash or lottery tickets; IQ does neither today. Felipe added a third ask: **multiple gift-card brands** ("what if I won an Amazon gift card or a Walmart gift card"), which Andrei put on the back burner as a different question about integrating other processors. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
  - PAX supports split only because entry is manual: "if the voucher from Motor Goose says $10, the cashier may input 5 on the card, verify the card to give it to player and give $5 as lottery tickets." Andrei argued IQ could split **without** manual entry and stay auditable. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
  - Juan's read on why locations want split: it lets them wash their hands — "if it's $100 cash out and we put 50 on the card, we're only going to reconcile the $50. In our world, that's all that happened. The other 50, that's on them." Val: "GLC can absolutely audit… These are your payouts. This amount should be on card. Everything else is [expletive]"; Juan: "but they don't want to audit." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
- **Competitor flows named.** **EasyLynx** (transcribed "Easylink"/"easy links") does replay "through a Tito ticket printed in the back end"; **Fiery Pay** does it "through sending credits to a machine number one or two or three," which Juan called "so dangerous" and Val "the wrong thing." Juan added that Felipe had heard "a lot of people are buying off on easy links and it's picking up traction." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
- **The QR / e-wallet direction — agreed in principle, flow not locked.** All four converged on ports in every machine plus a QR-code or barcode wallet the player carries. Val: "that something is installing [ports] into every machine"; Juan: "And the QR code, it lets you move from machine to machine." Val called a QR/barcode temporary wallet "a hot idea. I'm all about it." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
  - **Val's preferred flow:** keep the bill acceptor on the machine, play, and issue the e-wallet on cash-out — "approach the machine, have a bill acceptor, put money inside the bill acceptor, start a game, and on the cash out, get an e-wallet. That would be my desirable solution." Carrier medium: a printed ticket, or NFC / a QR shown on the player's phone — but "that requires a presentation of a personal communication device, which most players hesitate" for fear of giving up personal information. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
  - **Andrei's variant:** a simple standalone printer in the gaming area (a "black box" with one button) issuing a **zero-value** QR ticket the player then tops up at a bill validator. Val rejected it: "It's not going to work… you initiate first purchase from a recycler or a cashier. First, you need a wallet." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
  - **Felipe's variant, on relationship grounds:** issue the wallet at the counter on cash-out, because "for a lot of masters, that interaction with players is very, very important. And that's why they don't like kiosks," with "90% of locations… convenience stores or gas stations" needing to upsell. Juan agreed: "the kiosk disconnects the player from the cashier and the ability to upsell them on anything else." Felipe also cited the rule that gaming "cannot be more than 50%… of their sales." No flow was locked. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
  - Constraints raised against printers on machines: Juan — "There's not a printer located in every game in Georgia" and "30% of those machines in Georgia do not even support a printer from a technical standpoint." Juan also observed that Andrei's black-box printer "is the FlexPay kiosk without the card dispenser. We already have that," and Andrei's counter was cost: "kiosk costs 8, $9,000 and flex IQ cost 9 times less" — plus the space saving (Felipe). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
  - **Port vs SMIB, restated.** Andrei asked whether SMIB could be integrated into the port; Val: "you don't need to, because the port is elaborate version of SMIB… the port is the SMIP plus the optical reader plus the display." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
- **Mutha Goose is losing ground.** Asked whether Georgia locations exist without Mutha Goose, Felipe: yes — "those that have… kiosk and and using dual SAS, they don't have model groups." Val: "And Mother Goose is losing locations… eventually, we'll be out of business unless they… Do something different." Juan: "If you've got modern machines in a kiosk, you don't need Mother Goose." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
- **Recycler feedback — interest is high, security perception is not.** Val refused to detail the recycler ("the product is not ready"), but Andrei reported the show unit was "the slimmest device in the whole export," that on day two visitors ran the whole e-wallet flow unaided, and that Felipe's "board of directors of Gamora" (name as transcribed; the company is not otherwise identified) "were like… there were dollar signs in their eyes just seeing this device and how flawless it looks like." What they want changed: **thicker metal** — Val: "they would like to see thicker metal." Visitors also said to **remove the PIN code**: "let's remove the PIN code. It looks like we don't need it. Less friction… quicker money in, quicker money out" — the same conclusion Val reached three days later in [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-24-drop-pin-from-evoucher-flow.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
- **Ports drew good questions.** Asked which machines ports work with, "when they hear that it works with all the game machines currently in the market, it sounds really promising to them"; some asked whether JVL installs them or they do. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
- **A Texas test site is secured for the transactional products** — 20 machines taking Flex IQ, Flex ports and the Flex Dispenser, first week of October. Val parked the detail: "That's not for this conversation. That's the next thing." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]] Felipe reported little Texas interest at the Georgia show and suggested discussing a booth for **AMOA**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
- **Card ordering is the location's job.** Juan confirmed with Andrei: JVL orders the **first** pack when registering a location ("when we install, we will also have the first pack of cars"), shipped to Andrei's office and delivered with the hardware; "after that, it is their responsibility to refill it." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Georgia_Recap_and_JVL_Dollars-August-21-2026.md]]
- **§15.8:** clear — device, market and gift-card economics only; no named-person compensation.

## Aug 24 2026 — Flex-Port-Mini: a 2nd-SAS-only port, the QR eVoucher flow, and the local-network problem

On the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-24-flex-port-mini-capabilities.md|Aug-24 Flex-Port-Mini session]] (Russian; [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]], [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel Frolov]]) the team designed a new, cheap member of the Flex port family. Rulings: [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-24-flex-port-mini-via-second-sas-only.md]] (product), [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-24-free-hardware-fixed-monthly-fee-model.md]] (commercial model), [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-24-drop-pin-from-evoucher-flow.md]] (PIN).

- **What the Mini is.** Same footprint as today's port ("фудпринт такой же как… Сегодня"), no full screen, connected **only** to the machine's second SAS port, so the heavy bill-acceptor in-line wiring disappears. Genadi's summary of why that matters: "нету все этой сложной подключ вырывания. Этого билактора это же мейзинг" — no complicated cutting into the bill acceptor, which is amazing. Val: "Это это то же как сми, только он наружу торчит" — it is the same as a SMIB, only it sticks out (it has to, for the scanner). Genadi: "только наружу с маленьким экранчиком." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_Port_Mini_Capabilities_Design-August-24-2026.md]]
- **Player use-case (Val's, stated twice and then fixed as the spec).** A player hands the cashier, say, **$20** and receives a **QR code** — printed on a ticket, sent to a phone, or shown on screen to photograph — then presents it at any machine's optical scanner and all the money loads at once; on cash-out all of it comes off at once. Val listed **four** ways the cashier can issue it: print a ticket; scan any reusable plastic card carrying a unique barcode (a purely **local** carrier — Pavel: it cannot carry the server URL); send to a phone number; or display a QR to photograph. Pavel saw only the display-and-photograph route as realistic; Val pushed back that printing is fine because "принтеры у всех логичных сегодня есть." A fourth option they agreed on: the location pre-prints **blank** codes and just scans one. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_Port_Mini_Capabilities_Design-August-24-2026.md]]
  - Val's aside on incentives: the player can be pushed toward the phone route with promo credit — "если ты будешь пользоваться своим телефоном и тебе дадим промогрэт." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_Port_Mini_Capabilities_Design-August-24-2026.md]]
  - Pavel's objection to a "show me the balance, then cancel" feature: Val ruled it out — "все деньги ты заглатываешь или все деньги ты выдаёшь" — and offered the phone-scan balance check instead. Pavel: "Если хочешь посмотреть статус его лета, иди на терминал и смотри там." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_Port_Mini_Capabilities_Design-August-24-2026.md]]
- **Cash still enters through the operator's own bill acceptor** as it does today; the Mini takes the pay-out off the second SAS as an **AFT transaction** to the wallet via "минисасссервер, который будет крутиться на флексайти" — a mini SAS server running on Flex IQ (Pavel). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_Port_Mini_Capabilities_Design-August-24-2026.md]]
- **The local network is the main unsolved problem.** The ports need a local network to reach Flex IQ / the server so vouchers can be validated; today that is Ethernet plus a router installed at every location. Val: "Без локальной сети мы не можем вализировать… локальная сеть нужна"; Pavel: "Да вот это точно." Options, and what was decided about them, are on the product decision page. Genadi's observation that Mutha Goose already runs short CAT5 links machine-to-machine in a chain is why Val prefers reusing that wiring. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_Port_Mini_Capabilities_Design-August-24-2026.md]]
- **Two-stage rollout.** Stage 1 = Mini + Flex-Port with wired CAT5 to each machine on the existing protocol. Stage 2 (not committed) = drop Flex IQ and let any mobile device with a cashier app issue vouchers, which Pavel warned needs a modem in each SMIB port and puts the cost back. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_Port_Mini_Capabilities_Design-August-24-2026.md]]
- **Val's tempo.** He asked whether the board could be laid out "завтра утром"; Pavel said the code would not be ready by then but Val insisted the board layout would be. Val then asked what he had missed, because "я хочу сесть за бульдозер сейчас" — he wants to sit down at the bulldozer now. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_Port_Mini_Capabilities_Design-August-24-2026.md]]
- **§15.8:** clear — component, device and market economics only; no named-person compensation.

## Aug 25 2026 — the recycler is too small for a 25-machine Texas site: a stackable CPI unit, and Andrei's no-recycler topology

On an [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-25-texas-install-recycler-sourcing-cpi.md|Aug-25 call]] (~10:39 AM ET, English) [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] briefed [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi Peari]] on a Texas install of Flex-Ports and a Flex Recycler (market side on [[wiki/projects/texas-used-machine-distribution.md]], Aug 25). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Texas_Install_Recycler_Sourcing_CPI-August-25-2026.md]]

- **The use case.** "a customer approaches Flex Recycler, inserts money, gets a QR code… traverses through the machines back and forth as he wishes and comes back to the recycler… Cashes out." Flex-Port "is working"; Val wants it on the machine's second SAS too. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Texas_Install_Recycler_Sourcing_CPI-August-25-2026.md]]
- **Why the current recycler fails here.** "a small recycler will not work… not enough uh cash going through this." Val wants "more recycling cassettes" and "larger capacity, maybe fifty sixty mills instead of twenty five or thirty" (unit as spoken; not explained). The present product "is just not configurable to us. Because they designate the largest recycling cassette for the smallest denomination, and they don't want to reconfigure it" (vendor not named; it reads like the CPI drum-order rule recorded Jul 31 — inference). Denominations stay "One, five, ten and twenty". [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Texas_Install_Recycler_Sourcing_CPI-August-25-2026.md]]
- **The CPI lead.** Val met "CPI the marketing guy" at "The show in Georgia" (by context the GAMOA show — inference), who said CPI has a product that would "probably fit our description"; the name is unknown. Val understands it is "stackable. That you could use either four cassettes or six cassettes or eight cassettes", so capacity can grow with the location. Genadi said CPI "positioned it for venting" (vending); Val thought "large checkout counters. Like ah Walmart's and Kroger's". Val wants product information and the SDK — "we already have everything signed with CPI". Whether its SDK and protocol match what JVL has integrated is unknown. Detail on [[wiki/organizations/crane-cpi.md]] (Aug 25). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Texas_Install_Recycler_Sourcing_CPI-August-25-2026.md]]
- **Genadi's push-back: cassettes versus cash box.** "more cassettes, it doesn't help you because you fill up cassettes… I think you need a huge capacity the cash box, not the cassettes." Val disagreed ("I could put all the twenties into recycling cassettes"), then accepted the limit: a cash box is "roughly a thousand bills" (Val: "twelve hundred"; Genadi: "nine hundred"). Val cannot say how much capacity is enough "until we start getting our feet wet in business". [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Texas_Install_Recycler_Sourcing_CPI-August-25-2026.md]]
- **JCM stays in play.** Genadi: "in this terms JCM product is better". Val: "Let's continue working with JCM, but I also want to try the new C[PI]… and I don't want to wait." See [[wiki/organizations/jcm.md]] (Aug 25). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Texas_Install_Recycler_Sourcing_CPI-August-25-2026.md]]
- **A third vendor.** Val: "we also have a product from IT" (as transcribed; vendor not identified). Genadi: its "cradle port technology product… looks like a toy… all flimsy plastic". [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Texas_Install_Recycler_Sourcing_CPI-August-25-2026.md]]
- **Andrei's alternative topology — floated, then parked.** "Yesterday Andrei proposed another topographic solution": Flex-IQ at the counter, Flex-Ports on each machine, and "one standalone… tittle [TITO] printer in its own enclosure" — a slim, large-paper-capacity body placed close to the machines — for locations with no recycler, where cash goes in through each machine's bill acceptor. *(Andrei is by context [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]], JVL's transactional product manager, who presented a similar slim floor-printer option on Sept 2 — inference; the notes' full name is not in the recording.)* Val's objection is safety and disputes at a shared printer: two players cash out, and "I am stronger than you are. I'll get the living crap out of you"; Genadi: "or somebody else grab your ticket who is beside the printer". Printing at the cashier through IQ avoids that but means the player must "leave the machine and go to the cashier" to add money. Val: "recycler satisfies all the angles for me. Especially recycler, which acts as ATM machine". Outcome: "Let's park it." Average site size in this exchange: "average six machines per location" (Val). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Texas_Install_Recycler_Sourcing_CPI-August-25-2026.md]]
- **Notes divergence.** The notes give Genadi the CPI task outright; in the recording Val says "Let's connect with CPI" and Genadi's own stated task is Emmanuel. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Texas_Install_Recycler_Sourcing_CPI-August-25-2026.md]]
- **§15.8:** clear on substance; Val's personal remarks about his dealings with Andrei are not recorded.

## Aug 25 2026 (Epic) — the "final destination" pitched to a master, and the Access system it is measured against

At the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-25-epic-amusement-kenny-patel-visit.md|Aug-25 Epic Amusement visit]] (~11:11 AM ET, English) [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] walked [[wiki/people/kenny-patel.md|Kenny Patel]] and his banker through the transactional plan, one day before the Narayani visit. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]]

**The catalogue as named to the customer:** Flex Port, slot machine interface boards, Flex IQ ("a point of sale for… location"), Flex Pay ("a kiosk system"), Flex Dispenser ("dispenses cash like an ATM machine"), and a new "Redemption Center". [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]]

**The final destination — the recycler as ATM.**
- The player puts cash into a recycler that "sits between the machines", gets a QR code or barcode, and moves freely between machines, each with a Flex Port ("an optical reader… nothing moves nothing… breaks"). At the end he returns to the recycler. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]]
- The conversions run "in the background": the win goes onto a Mastercard prepaid card, then a prize card, then the InComm "skill card… coming out in September", which is dispensed. Inserting that card, "the recycler acts as a licensed ATM. I must have an ATM license, and I must be hooked up to a processing bank". [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]]
- **Why it pays: no refill.** Recycling cassettes ("like a tape recorder") hold the payout float; a drop cassette below holds "the leftover. This your profit". "You only deal with 10% of the overall cash." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]]
- **Target price "between nine and twelve thousand dollars"**, depending on capacity — "We still need to get there." A bare Fujitsu or NCR dispenser alone costs JVL "at three thousand five hundred dollars" and up. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]]
- **Card supply:** "We're making arrangements for income to ship directly and it fits into our recycler directly." *(The next day InComm said its cards ship to the location owner — see Aug 26 below. Recorded, not reconciled.)* [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]]
- **Who should own it:** "the natural fit is an operator for an ATM business rather than a location"; JVL can sell to either, and would service ATMs on contract. On existing store ATM contracts: "Leave that ATM sitting there… call it a recycling recycling redemption center." On the regulator: "Georgia Lottery has no jurisdiction over ATMs", but "I will never get in between you and Georgia Lottery Corporation." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]]
- **Bill acceptors.** "in my opinion, the best scenario is no printer, no bill acceptor" and "the more profitable way of operating is removing bill acceptors" — but "if you want to keep a bill acceptor at the machine, go ahead and keep it." Kenny: "It's not going to work in Georgia." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]]
- **The framing:** satisfy the master (ROI, little service), the location (players never bother the attendant) and the player (move between machines "uninhibited" and "in privacy"). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]]

**The competitor Epic is weighing: Access.** Kenny described it (transcribed "access", "XFS", "XOS", "extra system"): it removes the Mutha Goose and puts "all in one computer" at the counter; a ticket is redeemed there to free play, lottery or a gift card that works at any ATM (his example: a $5 fee on $55); and it gives month-by-month redemption reports for lottery inspectors. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]] Val's answer: that is JVL's **minimum entry** — Flex IQ on the counter, "you don't even need to remove MudaGoose. We actually connect two muda gooses with our terminal", with no rewiring, issuing redemption cards and giving per-machine and monthly reports, because "as of July first… locations are obligated… to convert winnings to the prepaid mastercard." He called counter-sent credits the old Belgian "telephone system" of the early 1980s: "a player wants to be anonymous." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]] After the meeting Felipe said the gas station on the corner runs "the access… the whole thing" alongside JVL machines. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]]

**Also said at lunch:** a contact rendered "Andreas Vaia", said to be "working now with recyclers", asked to come in the next morning (name unverified; not resolved). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]]

**Notes vs transcript.** The derived notes put the minimum-entry system first; in the recording Val gives the final destination first and the entry system only after Kenny pushes back. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Epic_Amusement_Kenny_Patel_Visit-August-25-2026.md]]

**§15.8:** device and market economics only.

## Aug 26 2026 — the product scope declared to InComm, the first live location, and a bigger CPI recycler nobody has a library for

Two separate Aug-26 calls touch this page.

**From the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-26-incomm-skill-pay-atm-guidance.md|InComm follow-up]] (3:00 PM ET):**

- **Only two transactional products go to Georgia in the next 12 months: Flex IQ and Flex Port.** Val, asked by Jason Hall to name every product JVL is bringing to the market: "In the foreseeable future, 12 months from today, we're seeing two products, **Flex IQ and Flex Port**" — Flex IQ described as "the **tabletop POS**", Flex Port as mounted **on the outside** of a machine (Val first said "inside of a machine"; [[wiki/people/juan-martinez.md|Juan]] corrected him and Val accepted: "I meant, I meant in the machine outside dashboard"). Full ruling on [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-26-certify-only-flex-iq-and-flex-port-with-incomm.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_InComm_Skill_Pay_ATM_Guidance-August-26-2026.md]]
- **Every product now carries a UX-certification gate before it can activate InComm cards** — UX screenshots plus a video of the full operation, reviewed by InComm; and for card-dispensing kiosks a terms-and-conditions kit to apply first. Any later interface change repeats the loop with a video, a test receipt and a test card. Detail on [[wiki/organizations/incom.md]] (Aug-26). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_InComm_Skill_Pay_ATM_Guidance-August-26-2026.md]]
- **The first real customer location launches on POS with the black Prize Pay card** ("in a matter of a week or so", Andrei Klimovich), because Skill Pay is not yet available in the wrapped POS form. Skill Pay's wrapped cards arrive **late September**; kiosk cards **early September**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_InComm_Skill_Pay_ATM_Guidance-August-26-2026.md]]
- **The recycler/ATM branch of the device catalog is blocked on licensing, and InComm has ruled against pairing it with a COAM kiosk.** Val confirmed JVL is working on an "**ATM Redemption Service**" but that it "cannot be implemented. Without us being licensed as an **ATM operator**. Which requires a bank." The constraint InComm imposed — no cash out of a COAM redemption device, and no coordinated kiosk+ATM pair from the same entity — is on [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-26-no-cash-from-coam-redemption-device-for-skill-pay.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_InComm_Skill_Pay_ATM_Guidance-August-26-2026.md]]

**From the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-26-alignment-planning-nova-september-releases.md|morning alignment call]] (7:40 AM ET, Russian):**

- **A bigger-capacity recycler is being sourced from CPI, and its library will not work.** Val told [[wiki/people/genadi-peari.md|Genadi]] to pick a different recycler from **CPI** — one with a large capacity that "может расти в капасаре" (can grow in capacity) — and believes Genadi has already ordered or is ordering it. [[wiki/people/pavel.md|Pavel]], who spoke to Genadi about it the day before, named the model only vaguely: "**это какойто b nr или BNP помоему модель BNR**" — some BNR or BNP, he thinks BNR — and reported Genadi's verdict: "**он уже сказал, что нет, это будет несовместим с нашей библиотекой**" (incompatible with our library), though he was promised the needed code/library. *(Model recorded as spoken — "BNR or BNP, he thinks BNR" — not asserted.)* [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Alignment_Planning_NOVA_and_Sept_Releases-August-26-2026.md]]
- **Pavel's precedent: they will have to reverse-engineer it, as they did on ARM.** "мы это делали для арм платформы. И мы, по сути, в чистом виде библиотеку не использовали. Мы полностью переписали то, что они нам прислали… Полностью сделали реверс инжиниринг" — for the ARM platform they used no vendor library as-shipped, rewrote everything sent to them and fully reverse-engineered it. "нам готового решения не будет" — there will be no ready-made solution. He added that reverse-engineering **with Claude goes quickly and is easy to check**; the real difficulty is that "**удалённо с этим сложно работать**" — it is hard to do remotely, with the physical device elsewhere. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Alignment_Planning_NOVA_and_Sept_Releases-August-26-2026.md]]
- **The remote-work problem, unsolved.** Val asked whether a more reliable **development station** could be built — better internet, some video interaction — and noted the more technical [[wiki/people/sebastian.md|Sebastian]] is arriving in Georgia. Pavel's counter: you can sit a person there and tell them "всунь деньгу… теперь там должно выехать сколькото купюр посчитай их" (insert the money, now some notes should come out, count them) — possible, "но это лишний человек. **Робота у нас нету удалённого**" (that is an extra person; we have no remote robot). Val: "я не вижу пока другого пути" — he sees no other path yet; the device will be shipped to Minsk, and even the fastest shipping is "**гдето 30 45 дней**." Both men parked it. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Alignment_Planning_NOVA_and_Sept_Releases-August-26-2026.md]]
- **§15.8:** clear — device, card-programme and shipping economics only; no named-person compensation.

## Aug 27 2026 — Flex-IQ must be able to switch clearing houses without re-approving the software

On the seven-minute [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-27-flex-iq-multiple-clearinghouse-selection.md|Aug-27 call]] ([[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]], [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]]) Val added one capability to Flex-IQ: at device registration, a **selection of which clearing house does the clearance** — not [[wiki/organizations/incom.md|InComm]] by default. Full ruling, including the per-device scope and the explicit not-in-the-MVP and don't-contact-other-clearinghouses-yet limits: [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-27-flex-iq-multiple-clearinghouses-selectable-per-device.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Flex_IQ_Multiple_Clearinghouse_Selection-August-27-2026.md]]

- **The device-architecture consequence** is the one Val stated as the hard requirement: "We just need to have this capability that we could easily switch on a fly. **I don't want to be re-approving the software. I don't want to be designing the software again.**" Scope is **per device, not per location** — "So let's say IQ is something that needs to be cleared. We could have, let's say, a **self-attending device**. That's a different story." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Multiple_Clearinghouse_Selection-August-27-2026.md]]
- **The field use-case Andrei brought back from the day before (not what Val was asking for).** Some locations run more than one card provider at once — the transcript's example is "**FirePay** plus income" (rendered "**Fiery Pay**" a few lines later; JVL's known competitor of that name is on the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-12-fiery-pay-competitive-analysis.md|Aug-12 competitive analysis]]). If a player wins **$8,000** and one supplier's daily cap — "let's say **5K**" — is reached through InComm, "the location still has an opportunity to issue the rest of **3K**" through the other. This is the same two-provider pattern Jason Hall described on Aug 12 (credit limits pushing locations to sign a second provider), now seen from the device side. Val's answer: "**The goal is to have that choice, but I'm not necessarily saying that it should be the location that chooses it.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Multiple_Clearinghouse_Selection-August-27-2026.md]]
- **A separate 4:00 PM session the same day** was called on "the additional features that you are proposing for **printer and the dispenser**" (Val to Andrei; Andrei proposed 4 PM, Val agreed and said he had sent the invite). No content from that session is in this batch. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Flex_IQ_Multiple_Clearinghouse_Selection-August-27-2026.md]]
- **§15.8:** clear — clearinghouse/daily-cap economics only; no named-person compensation.

## Aug 26–27 2026 — how the transactional suite is actually pitched to an operator, twice in two days

Two customer visits ([[wiki/meetings/2026-08-26-narayani-amusements-trade-in-negotiation.md|Aug-26 Narayani]], [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-27-halcyon-amusements-recycler-pitch.md|Aug-27 Halcyon]]) show the product catalogue as it leaves Val's mouth in front of a buyer — worth recording because it is an early customer-facing statement of the bundle. *(Corrected on back-fill: the Aug-25 Epic visit above is one day earlier.)*

- **The bundle, named to the customer.** "The transactional equipment consists of a number of things. First of all, it's a **point-of-sale terminal at the attendance counter**… it consists also of **ports that are mounted in the machine**. We also have future equipment… We're currently going through some **licensing provisions** which will actually turn your cost center, which you have today, into your **profit center** by introducing a product which we'll call **ATM Recycler**. So in effect, you will be able to act as an **ATM operator** and instantly… Payout from the ATM." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Narayani_Amusements_Trade_In_Negotiation-August-26-2026.md]]
- **The port, described in plain words:** "A modem like a like a what do you call this port? It's? Like, uh it's like an **extra screen**. It's uh has an **optical reader QR code**, and you'll get so each machine will have that. And you also getting a **POS terminal**." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Narayani_Amusements_Trade_In_Negotiation-August-26-2026.md]]
- **The price Val puts on it in a trade: "Each unit is one thousand dollars… I'm including all of this in the price."** Consistent with the $1,000-POS + credit-note go-to-market recorded on Feb-10 2026 above, and with the JVL-Dollars logic on [[wiki/topics/jvl-machine-pricing-strategy.md]]. He also made installation, not evaluation, the condition of the bigger machine deal — "**Just install it. Don't show it.**" See [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-26-narayani-three-machine-d27-trade-in-pilot.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Narayani_Amusements_Trade_In_Negotiation-August-26-2026.md]]
- **What the operator says he actually wants** is narrower than the suite: his big locations "like to just stick with the old system where they have to do manual replay. All they want is how this gift cards are being issued… They have ATM access to it." And the buying rule: "Customers always look for one thing — they don't want to spend money out of their pocket." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Narayani_Amusements_Trade_In_Negotiation-August-26-2026.md]]
- **The competing devices already in those locations**, per the operator: a gift-card program at "**fifty dollars a month**… subscription" for the POS, plus hardware Val put at "three to five hundred dollars. Plus each machine has a piece of hardware"; a JVL voice cited "around **seven hundred and fifty dollars for the device**" for one rival, with an in-machine connector "like **four hundred dollars** in change." The rival product name is transcribed "try and pay" and is **not resolved**; the derived notes read the primary provider as **InComm via PAX**. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Narayani_Amusements_Trade_In_Negotiation-August-26-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Narayani_Amusements_Trade_In_Negotiation-August-26-2026.md]]
- **Aug 27 — the recycler pitched against a pre-loaded ATM.** Against a Texas operator running "a **PTI**, and then a **PTI Sentry** system with the kiosk" whose ATM staff pre-load by hand, Val's pitch was the labour and shrinkage, not the hardware: "You take the money from the machine. You go in the back room. You **sort** it… There's probably some **shrinkage** in between… You now have to go and fit the money into each bin. And that's expensive. Each cassette. **We do it automatically.**" The recycler answer: the player's buy-in funds the payout, "you never preloaded… **There's no shrinkage because the player is using their own cash**," and staff touch only "the **drop cash box**… even that comes lockable, so they could swap it instantly. A different cassette." A live demo had been given in the room ("**Andre** showed you. He put money in. This money is going to recycling"). *(Which Andre/Andrei is not stated.)* [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Halcyon_Amusements_Recycler_Pitch_and_Lottery_Notice-August-27-2026.md]]
- **The operator's reply was a request for pricing at scale:** "So give me some numbers on this. It's a good idea. I've got four hundred locations in Texas." See [[wiki/projects/texas-used-machine-distribution.md]] (Aug-27). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Halcyon_Amusements_Recycler_Pitch_and_Lottery_Notice-August-27-2026.md]]
- **A free three-month Flex-IQ pilot was offered and not closed.** An unnamed JVL voice offered to run IQ against **MudaGoose** at no charge for three months, adding sports later and eventually dropping MudaGoose; the operator offered a **Lilburn** location. No date or confirmation follows, and the derived notes omit the item entirely — carried as an open question on [[wiki/organizations/halcyon-amusements.md]]. *(MudaGoose is rendered "Montegoos"/"Nootegoo" in the transcript.)* [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Halcyon_Amusements_Recycler_Pitch_and_Lottery_Notice-August-27-2026.md]]
- **A dispensing fault was being worked in the room**, cause not settled: "If it's a software issue, which means our issue, or if it's a **dispensing** issue. Dispensing definitely has enough five dollars" / "Probably a software. Could be. But let's test it afterwards… I'm pretty sure it's not mechanical." No device or ticket number is given. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Halcyon_Amusements_Recycler_Pitch_and_Lottery_Notice-August-27-2026.md]]
- **§15.8:** clear — device prices, subscription fees and cash-handling economics only; no named-person compensation.

## Aug 27 2026 (ARC coffee) — the entry-level product pitched to a master: a counter POS plus Flex-Ports on SAS 2

At the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-27-arc-coffee-fleet-swap-and-transactional-pitch.md|Aug-27 coffee at ARC Gaming]] (~9:45 AM ET, English) [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] set the kiosk aside — "I showed you something that freaked out the legislators" — and pitched the smallest version of the suite. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ARC_Coffee_Fleet_Swap_and_Transactional_Pitch-August-27-2026.md]]

- **What it is.** "an entry level product which is, A point of sale, uh coupled with uh a flex port… a player system mounted inside of the machine, and this system connects to SaaS two protocol. The SaaS two port." The POS is "a small unit that is desktop mount counter mount. Where an attendant can convert the winnings onto a prepaid card", because "every location in Georgia because of the new legislation needs to provide a player with a conversion into a prepaid card." An ARC voice named the device class: "Pax device." *(The Aug-26 InComm call had the Flex Port on the outside dashboard; here Val says "inside of the machine". Recorded as said.)* [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ARC_Coffee_Fleet_Swap_and_Transactional_Pitch-August-27-2026.md]]
- **Price: "a thousand dollar per machine retail"** — and then "Don't worry about the price… Stimulate a discussion on you being a poster child for my product." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ARC_Coffee_Fleet_Swap_and_Transactional_Pitch-August-27-2026.md]]
- **Against Fiery Pay: who carries the money.** "The premise of their business is to scream across the room and say 'attendant.' Machine number five, $20." JVL's premise: "the carrier of the medium should be a player" — the attendant or "an anonymous kiosk" issues "an electronic temporary wallet… a card… a piece of paper… a prepaid card." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ARC_Coffee_Fleet_Swap_and_Transactional_Pitch-August-27-2026.md]]
- **The QR flow, step by step.** A button on a printer "prints you a QR code. This QR code is meaningless"; the player presents it at a machine's port, adds $20 through the bill acceptor, plays, and pays out onto the same QR; it then works at "Another machine JVL machine, Banilla machine IGT machines, like in Wonder machines… We hook it up directly to SAS two port"; at the counter "It says this QR code has two hundred dollars. Would you like to issue a card?… we validate it through [InComm] and issue a card." Options: a PIN "depending on the level of security"; a printer to "convert it to TITO"; or a blank InComm card whose barcode "becomes your carrier". An ARC voice summed it up: "electronically you can add funds and move the funds around on that QR code." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ARC_Coffee_Fleet_Swap_and_Transactional_Pitch-August-27-2026.md]]
- **Single-SAS machines: a pulse fix.** "probably twenty percent of the market that still has single SaaS port that we can intercept the signal. So basically unplug a bill acceptor, plug it into our own port… Pick up pulses. Shitty solution… our preference today is SaaS too." (See the open contradiction on the size of this segment.) [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ARC_Coffee_Fleet_Swap_and_Transactional_Pitch-August-27-2026.md]]
- **A cheaper version still.** "I can remove these flex ports and just give you a QR code optical QR code reader… probably convertible at the rate of 75 bucks per machine." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ARC_Coffee_Fleet_Swap_and_Transactional_Pitch-August-27-2026.md]]
- **The fee.** "we need a forty nine dollar per month charge… [InComm] offers is a dollar for… validated transaction. So we don't want that dollar. We're happy to give up the dollar to either you or location" — "Per location", an ARC voice added. Model on [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-24-free-hardware-fixed-monthly-fee-model.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ARC_Coffee_Fleet_Swap_and_Transactional_Pitch-August-27-2026.md]]
- **Against Access: NFC.** ARC compared it with "access" (a players-club card and a device in the machine). Val: "they have the NFC solution, not an optical solution… I don't like the NFC solution… it's a dollar per user, per location, per day… it amounts to hundreds of thousands of dollars." His claim: "we're the only solution today that will allow you to on a fly to choose a TITO or a QR code medium attendant based or no attendant with kiosk." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ARC_Coffee_Fleet_Swap_and_Transactional_Pitch-August-27-2026.md]]
- **Reporting without a CMS.** Asked about a CMS: "We actually were approached by a route management company. They want our API"; Val's view: an export file into "Tableau" or a spreadsheet, or "an API or an MCP to our server… whoever sells management software, I don't see them in business much longer." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ARC_Coffee_Fleet_Swap_and_Transactional_Pitch-August-27-2026.md]]
- **Recycler capacity, as Val gave it.** "Jcm I think is thirty nodes, and CPI is one hundred twenty nodes per denomination. And CPI has I think up to six recycling cassettes" ("nodes" as transcribed; Val's own "I think"). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ARC_Coffee_Fleet_Swap_and_Transactional_Pitch-August-27-2026.md]]
- **The ATM argument, again.** "Most of the locations in Georgia that I visited have ATMs today… it robs the operator from profit because they're paying stupid money for refill… Where my system is free." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ARC_Coffee_Fleet_Swap_and_Transactional_Pitch-August-27-2026.md]]
- **Next step:** a demo at JVL's office the following Monday (Aug 31). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_ARC_Coffee_Fleet_Swap_and_Transactional_Pitch-August-27-2026.md]]
- **§15.8:** clear — device, fee and market economics only.

## Aug 31 2026 — "there's no money in selling kiosks": the fee sheet that set the recurring-revenue premise

At the [[wiki/meetings/2026-08-31-dixie-amusement-kiosk-wait-and-see.md|Aug-31 Dixie Amusement visit]] [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] took apart a **Fiery Pay fee schedule** handed to him by [[wiki/people/salim-khambawala.md|Salim Khambawala]] and drew the product conclusion out loud. The full arithmetic — activation, dormancy, lost-card and transaction fees, the stranded-balance float, and the ~$5bn/15% market estimate — is on [[wiki/topics/georgia-prize-card-processor-economics.md]]. Three lines belong here because they set the terms this whole catalogue is built to. [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Dixie_Amusement_Kiosk_Wait_and_See_and_Market_Economics-August-31-2026.md]]

- **The kiosk is not the business.** "**how do we make money? Selling kiosks. There's no money in selling kiosks. You cannot make money**… we don't believe in kiosks. **We're showing kiosks, but we don't think that people should be buying kiosks.** There's no value in [them]… **even that technology is changing now.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Dixie_Amusement_Kiosk_Wait_and_See_and_Market_Economics-August-31-2026.md]]
- **The premise stated as a rule:** "**The value is in recurring revenue. This is where the value is. Unless we can get recurring revenue, there's no point for us.**" This is the same premise as the free-hardware / fixed-monthly-fee model on [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-24-free-hardware-fixed-monthly-fee-model.md]], stated to a customer rather than internally. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Dixie_Amusement_Kiosk_Wait_and_See_and_Market_Economics-August-31-2026.md]]
- **And the condition attached to placing any of it:** exclusivity in the location, MudaGoose removed, JVL rewiring, its own POS replacing PAX, Flex ports in the machines, and a retained remote shut-off — the ruling on [[wiki/decisions/2026-08-31-transactional-equipment-only-with-location-exclusivity.md]]. Two days later the shut-off half of that became "a hard requirement" on the Sept-2 call below. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Dixie_Amusement_Kiosk_Wait_and_See_and_Market_Economics-August-31-2026.md]]
- **§15.8:** clear — card fees and device/market economics only; no named-person compensation.

## Sept 1 2026 — the "Lego bricks" pitch: the full catalogue walked end-to-end in front of a customer

At the [[wiki/meetings/2026-09-01-double-down-gaming-visit.md|Sept-1 Double Down Gaming visit]] [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]] demonstrated the suite live and in sequence, and [[wiki/people/val-levitan.md|Val]] priced it. It is the most complete customer-facing walkthrough of the catalogue in the corpus. *(Speaker 5 runs the demo and Val addresses him as "Andrew"; reading Speaker 5 as Andrei is an inference from role and from the derived notes.)* [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]

### The upgrade ladder, as pitched

Andrei's own framing: "I know it's a lot of different stuff right now, but **think about that as Lego bricks. You can build up and set up for every single location. You can start with a very simple solution, just to comply with regulations today, so it works with [MudaGoose]. Later, if you're ready to upgrade it, you upgrade it with ports and you get replayability. And later, card dispenser, and you can remove it. No cashier time needed anymore.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]

1. **Step one — card activation by barcode, on a drop-on-top device.** "normally, cards from [InComm]… **need to be activated by reading a barcode. The cashier will just take this part, scan the barcode, and it's activated**… The card is activated and it's handed to the player. With a barcode reader, **the receipt will be printed using the same printer that was already** [there]." Described as "**a drop on top of a [MudaGoose]**." This is the same capability that became a formal ruling the next day — [[wiki/decisions/2026-09-02-activate-incomm-cards-by-barcode-on-flex-iq.md]] — and it is worth noting it was being **demonstrated live to a customer the day before** that call. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]
2. **Step two — add Flex ports for cross-machine replay.** "The next step would be still using this device… but **in combination with Flex ports**. Think about that as a solution for the requirement you just mentioned, **when the player needs to move between machines.**" The flow, without a printer or laser on every machine: "**You go into scan mode. You confirm it with your pin code**… **You can transfer all or partial**" — and it is configurable per location: "**it's configurable, so it can be set up for each single location. No pin code needed. Switch off. Partial redemption.**" The demo itself: play ten dollars, "what happens in a regular ticket flow? **It will print out the ticket.** But here, **you just need to scan it again**… **Either to another machine and you transfer it, or you go right back to this device**… **so minus [a] cheaper fee for every game**." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]
3. **Step three — upgrade the kiosk with a card dispenser, and from September 15 the card redeems at an ATM.** "the next step for the location might be to **upgrade the kiosks**. It's not only printing our tickets to create new ones, but let's say **we integrate a card display** [dispenser]… **starting with September fifteenth**… **It is an ATM. It's still not reloadable, but it's an ATM**… **Now they can go to an ATM to redeem their cash.**" Beyond that, "somewhere next year… **It all depends on legislation.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]
4. **Step four — the recycler as the single point of entry.** "**think about that as a keyhole which can be modified with any** [accessory]… **accessibility through card dispenser, cash dispenser,** etc." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]

A JVL voice added the argument that is not about compliance: "**it's not just compliance. You avoid fraud by not manually putting** [in] whatever [the] location [says]. It is a solution to comply, but I believe at this stage **it's much more than that. The simplicity.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]

### Price, interface and mounting — stated to a customer

- **$1,000 for the full unit; a couple of hundred for a slim version; and it can be packaged with machines.** "**We are showing here a full unit. This is a thousand dollar unit. We can make this into a couple hundred dollar unit retail price, and we can also package it together with machines.** So we can do many things to make your life cheaper. **We can have you, if you want, install a printer or not. We can have you keep your validator or not. You make the rules.**" *(The $1,000 figure matches the Aug-26 Narayani statement above.)* [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]
- **It is JVL's own hardware, not licensed in.** "**It's all designed by us. It's ours. We're not buying it. We're not leasing it or licensing it. It's our unit.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]
- **SAS 2 is the connection; a bill-acceptor port is the fallback for pre-SAS machines, and JVL is not investing there.** "**this connects to SAS two**, or in the case of very old machines that don't have SAS, we can configure it. We can basically **connect it to a bill acceptor port and connect the bill acceptor onto here. But those machines are dying; they're going away. So it's not our priority. This unit connects directly to SAS two and connects to any machine.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]
- **The mounting dimensions are published openly — a deliberate choice.** "**We are releasing our dimensions openly and freely, so any operator could make a bracket, or we could make a bracket for you for any machine, and this fits a standard size.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]

### The recycler as a licensed closed-loop ATM

- **It is a fully licensed ATM, but looped to the machine.** Asked whether it is a closed system or talks to a processor network: "**This is a fully licensed ATM**" — then, when the operator said "**I don't want it to be an ATM. I want it to be a game**," Val narrowed it: "**it needs to be ATM licensed. It is ATM licensed**… **It's a closed loop ATM. It is looped only to the machine.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]
- **Buy-ins fund the payouts, and nobody ever refills it.** "in this device, we can put money in. **The reserve, the payout money, will remain in recycling cassettes. The drop cassette is what you collect, but the recycling cassettes also pay out the money, and you never have to refill it.** So **that nightmare of ATM where a location collects money, counts it, sorts it, puts it into dispensing cassettes — all of it is gone.**" And: "**those cassettes are lockable; they're not accessible.**" This is the same argument made at Halcyon on Aug 27, made here to an operator who is himself in the ATM business — Val's framing was "**we have a device that will remove the nightmare element out of the ATM and leave only the profit in the ATM, not the nightmare.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]
- **The hard constraint: the validator must come out.** "**If you are going with this solution, you must remove your validator. Otherwise, there is no refill**"; "**You want to run your location with bill acceptors, then you cannot have a recycler.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]

### Promo credits over the SAS promo meter — deliberately not advertised

A capability Val raised and immediately qualified: "**we're not really discussing this or talking about it much because we don't want to freak out the legislators.** As the technology progresses, **we can also have you send promo credits to your player**, and your player could come in and through [SAS] two port **play out the promo credits, which go to promo meter**, and then the winnings could now be wagered or paid out." How it stays clean: "**SAS has a promo meter separate from the accounting meter, and those promo credits cannot be redeemed. They can only go into the promo meter. They can be played. Whatever is won there can be redeemed, but not the promo credits.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]

### The carrier-agnostic doctrine, stated to a customer before the Sept-2 call

The carrier-medium argument that dominates the Sept-2 internal call below was delivered here first, to a buyer: [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]

> "**we are technology agnostic**… **this piece of paper is just an information carrier. It could be we could use this, or we could use a cigarette box with a barcode number, or we could use your phone as a carrier of information**… Display a QR code here on this screen, and with your phone, scan it, or print this from [MudaGoose]'s printer, and then scan it here. **To us, it makes no difference. Whatever carrier you think that you want your players to utilize in a location, to us, it makes no difference.**"

- **And the four parties it has to satisfy, ranked.** "**We understand that we need to satisfy three parties, or maybe four parties. You as master, a location, a legislator, and a player**… **you, or location, or legislator, is less important than the player. Because if the player doesn't like something, neither you, nor location, nor legislator, can enforce it, control it, or regulate it. The player has to be happy**… in the history of America, we know many examples of prohibition and regulation and overregulation, and **it never works because a player is the king.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]
- **The cost argument that follows it:** "to satisfy you, **we need to bring the cost down**… **your cost consists of service costs and direct material costs.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]
- **What the port does, in one line, against the incumbent:** "**This will basically do what [InComm] does with [PAX] right now, but in an automated format. If you add this, it will allow your players to play any machine.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Double_Down_Gaming_Operations_Review-September-1-2026.md]]

**§15.8:** clear — device prices, interface specifications and cash-handling economics only; no named-person compensation.

## Sept 1 2026 (afternoon) — the same catalogue sold twice more in one day, with prices, and the anti-duplication architecture spelled out

Two further customer visits the same day as the Double Down walkthrough above: [[wiki/meetings/2026-09-01-narayani-akshar-mega-hit-demo-and-trade-in.md|Narayani/Akshar at ~1:05 PM]] and [[wiki/meetings/2026-09-01-air-gaming-group-transactional-walkthrough.md|Air Gaming Group at ~3:00 PM]]. [[wiki/people/andrei-klimovich.md|Andrei Klimovich]] ran the demo in both — named in the room at Narayani ("**Andre… He's the product** [manager]"), and at Air Gaming identified from Val's "**This is Andrew as our product manager**" plus role, **an inference flagged on that stub**. Three things are new here: **firm unit prices spoken to customers**, the **compatibility and install claims**, and the **cloud-as-source-of-truth argument against ticket duplication**, which is the first time in the corpus the anti-fraud rationale for the cloud is stated end to end.

### The prices quoted, and the one discrepancy

| Item | Narayani/Akshar, ~1:05 PM | Air Gaming, ~3:00 PM |
|---|---|---|
| Flex port (in-machine, with screen) | "**Each flex port is nine hundred fifty dollars.** The one with the screen, the whole computer is nine hundred fifty dollars" | "the flex port, is **approximately a thousand dollars**" |
| Flex-IQ / counter device | "**Nine fifty, and this is a thousand.** Think about a thousand plus a thousand per machine" · "**this is a thousand dollar device**" | "**This unit is a thousand dollars**" |
| Kiosk, bill acceptor + printer, no dispenser | "the estimate price for the kiosk without ATM?" — "**Approximately five thousand**" | "without the dispenser, is **roughly five thousand dollars**" |
| Kiosk with cash dispenser | "**With the ATM of approximately twelve thousand**" | "**approximately twelve thousand dollars**" |
| Monthly | not quoted in this meeting | "**plus a ninety-dollar monthly charge for connection. Nine zero**… **It's ninety dollars per location**" |

[[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Narayani_Amusements_Mega_Hit_Demo_and_Trade_In-September-1-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Air_Gaming_Group_Transactional_Walkthrough-September-1-2026.md]]

**On the $950 vs "approximately a thousand" gap: not treated as a contradiction.** Val explicitly labelled the afternoon set as indicative — "**I'm giving you retail pricing, as though you're from the street and I'm selling you one unit**" — and the derived notes for that meeting repeat the "retail, explicitly" framing. The $950 figure is the precise one, stated twice and corrected upward from a mishearing in the room ("So you said the screen this one. It was nine hundred." — "**Nine fifty**"). [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Air_Gaming_Group_Transactional_Walkthrough-September-1-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Air_Gaming_Group_Transactional_Walkthrough-September-1-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Narayani_Amusements_Mega_Hit_Demo_and_Trade_In-September-1-2026.md]]

**A post-show hardware giveaway attached to a machine order.** "on a limited basis, after the show, we're offering a special where **if you buy X number of these machines, we can package the deal together with the machine, which will cost you nothing**" — and, crucially, not only for JVL cabinets: "**We'll give you six of these units plus a terminal for whatever machines you have.**" Installation is the customer's: "**You install it, but it's very simple. You'll give us the name of the machine. We'll give you the bracket, and you install that unit. This unit fits right in and connects to a SAS port.**" *(The number of machines is stated only as "X" in the transcript. The derived notes put the machine price at **$16,000**; that figure appears **only** in the notes and is recorded as the notes' reading.)* See [[wiki/topics/jvl-machine-pricing-strategy.md]]. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Narayani_Amusements_Mega_Hit_Demo_and_Trade_In-September-1-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Narayani_Amusements_Mega_Hit_Demo_and_Trade_In-September-1-2026.md]]

**And a release gate:** "**We're going through the approval process. This will be ready for installation after we get approval. Let us know how many pieces you want.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Narayani_Amusements_Mega_Hit_Demo_and_Trade_In-September-1-2026.md]]

### Compatibility, install and mounting — stated to a customer

- **All Class III manufacturers.** "It's compatible with **all class three manufacturers**, but if there will be some homemade manufacturer, I don't know… **Light and Wonder, IGT, Aristocrat, Novomatic.** There's a lot." *(The next clause — "All of it is compatible 100% which but Banilla" — is garbled: it may mean Banilla is included or that it is the exception. The derived notes read it as "plus Banilla." **Not resolved here.**)* [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Air_Gaming_Group_Transactional_Walkthrough-September-1-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Notes_Air_Gaming_Group_Transactional_Walkthrough-September-1-2026.md]]
- **Fully self-contained.** "**This is a fully encompassing integrated system. You don't need anything else for the system. You don't need an interface board. You don't need a power supply.** … This goes inside of **any class three machine, connects to SAS two port**, and it should work." Plus the reason for the form factor: "the reason it has a screen and the reason it plugs into the second SAS port is because **we take it from class three, from casino class three architecture**." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Air_Gaming_Group_Transactional_Walkthrough-September-1-2026.md]]
- **Brackets pre-designed, drawings published.** "**We provide it fully pre-designed in a custom bracket for you, with an instruction on how to install it for every single type**… so it's like **five minutes connection**," and "**We also give you the drawings for this, so you could design your own bracket. The other manufacturer should really give you the drawing of their opening.**" This is the same open-dimensions policy stated to Double Down that morning. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Air_Gaming_Group_Transactional_Walkthrough-September-1-2026.md]]
- **Old machines without a second SAS port have a fallback.** "**This is connected to the SAS port. We also have a version that lets you connect if you have old machines that don't have a second SAS port.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Narayani_Amusements_Mega_Hit_Demo_and_Trade_In-September-1-2026.md]]
- **One vendor, not three.** [[wiki/people/felipe-miranda.md|Felipe]]'s version of the pitch: with a kiosk today "you have need to call [EasyLink] and say no. That's with the [kiosk] manufacturer. **This is JVL who is doing the whole thing. So there's no need to call here and there. And it's connected to any of all the machines.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Narayani_Amusements_Mega_Hit_Demo_and_Trade_In-September-1-2026.md]]

### The cashier device and the kiosk, demonstrated

- **No manual entry, which is the whole point of the counter device.** "As a cashier, I will sign in with my personalized info… **I see this transaction already here immediately, and I don't need to input anything manually**… **This one is full automation**," and the same line at Narayani: "**The key advantage is that you don't need to do any manual work.**" Access levels are per-user, and the transaction history is available "either from the same device or you will have access to a **web dashboard**." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Air_Gaming_Group_Transactional_Walkthrough-September-1-2026.md]] [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Narayani_Amusements_Mega_Hit_Demo_and_Trade_In-September-1-2026.md]]
- **Card activation is by barcode**, with the receipt printed on the printer already in the location. The demo used a test code: "**Right now, I will show you with a test card, but in reality, it will work by reading the barcodes.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Air_Gaming_Group_Transactional_Walkthrough-September-1-2026.md]]
- **A dated card-product line was named and is partly garbled.** "This one is a **two-dollar** situation, paid by player, and this one is a **three-dollar** situation. **It will be introduced into the market on September fifteenth**," followed by "**This card is an ATM access card**, so this one is just a debit card… It communicates with [InComm]. **It's approved.**" The Sept-15 date matches the ATM-access card Andrei described to Double Down that morning; the two- and three-dollar figures are **not** clearly attached to anything in the transcript and no fee structure is asserted here. [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Air_Gaming_Group_Transactional_Walkthrough-September-1-2026.md]]
- **PIN is optional and per-location, and JVL never holds it.** "**you don't have to use the pin code. You could set it up without a pin code. If you apply a pin code, that's something we have no access to. This is strictly for the player. It's like losing cash.**" Alternative: "**You could scan the QR code with your phone, and then have it on your phone. That is more secure.**" [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Air_Gaming_Group_Transactional_Walkthrough-September-1-2026.md]]
- **The kiosk removes per-machine printers and, optionally, per-machine validators.** "**you basically don't need printers for every game machine**"; and going further, "**we can remove all validators, so it's minus the need to service those machines and count this cash**… we can remove all the [validators from the] machines and keep **one kiosk or couple of kiosks per location**, where players will actually input money and get this ticket." [[raw/Meeting_Transcript_Narayani_Amusements_Mega_Hit_Demo_and_Trade_In-September-1-2026.md]]
- **The ATM fee is the operator's own revenue.** "**you are the one who owns it. It's up to you to decide what will be the fee**… **And this fee is your revenue.**" Val added the customer-side arithmetic on the card the player receives: "**Two dollars, and they also pay when they put it into an ATM**… **plus there is a charge for inactivity.
